# Closing the hierarchy-to-process step without an independent UI assumption

Date: 2026-10-15.

Status: self-contained finite comparison inequality and a conditional implication. The signed trace hypothesis is NOT proved for the frozen BGS propagator. No priority, independent external refereeing, formal verification, or solution of BGS is claimed. The GitHub repository has not been modified.

## 1. Precise remaining hypothesis

Let U_N be any deterministic unitary N by N matrix, with phases theta_j in R/Z, allowing multiplicities. Let alpha be uniform on R/Z and define the periodic, unfolded counting measure

    Xi_(N,alpha) = sum_(j=1)^N sum_(l in Z) delta_(N(theta_j-alpha+l)).

Expectation below is over alpha only. In particular, for every bounded Borel set I,

    E Xi_(N,alpha)(I) = |I|.                                      (1)

For an integer tuple m_1+...+m_k=0, define

    D_U(m) = sum_(j_1,...,j_k distinct) exp(2 pi i sum_a m_a theta_(j_a)).

For a real nonnegative Schwartz function b with smooth compactly supported Fourier transform and sup b < 1, define

    b_N(t) = sum_(l in Z) b(N(t+l)),
    M_(k,N)^U(b) = (1/k!) integral_0^1
        sum_(j_1,...,j_k distinct) product_a b_N(theta_(j_a)-alpha) d alpha.

Set M_(0,N)=1. Exactly, M_(1,N)=H:=integral_R b(x) dx.

Let M_(k,N)^CUE(b) be the expectation of the same statistic for Haar U(N). Put

    Delta_(k,N)(b) = M_(k,N)^U(b) - M_(k,N)^CUE(b).

The reduced hypothesis is

    Delta_(k,N)(b) -> 0                                        (H_diag)

for every fixed k>=2 and every b in the class above. The full signed Heisenberg hypothesis of the project implies (H_diag). No rates uniform in k or in the Fourier bandwidth are assumed. The limits are always taken at fixed k and fixed b.

### Exact Fourier expression

Poisson summation and integration over alpha give

    M_(k,N)^U(b)
      = 1/(k! N^k) sum_(m_1+...+m_k=0)
              product_a b_hat(m_a/N) D_U(m_1,...,m_k).          (2)

This is a finite sum because b_hat has compact support. Its test weight is

    W_(k,b)(xi_1,...,xi_(k-1))
      = (1/k!) product_(a<k) b_hat(xi_a)
                    b_hat(-sum_(a<k)xi_a).

The absolute value in Delta is taken AFTER the complete signed sum. Individual trace products are not replaced by absolute values. If supp b_hat is contained in [-B,B], contributing trace indices still have size O_k(BN).

## 2. Exact CUE reference and its factorial bound

Define the Hermitian Toeplitz matrix, indexed by 0<=i,j<N,

    T_(N,b)(i,j) = (1/N) b_hat((i-j)/N).

It is positive semidefinite: for c in C^N,

    c* T_(N,b) c = integral_0^1 b_N(t)
                       |sum_j c_j exp(2 pi i j t)|^2 dt >= 0.

Also Tr T_(N,b)=H. For sufficiently large N (depending on fixed b), b_N<=1, so T_(N,b)<=I. Indeed, after choosing the nearest lift, all other terms of the periodization are bounded uniformly by a Schwartz tail O_A(N^-A), while sup b<1.

The CUE eigenphase density is the squared Fourier Vandermonde divided by N!. Expanding its two determinants and integrating each variable proves the finite identity

    E_CUE product_(j=1)^N (1-z b_N(theta_j))
                  = det(I-z T_(N,b)).                         (3)

Thus M_(k,N)^CUE(b) is the kth elementary symmetric polynomial in the nonnegative eigenvalues of T_(N,b). In particular

    0 <= M_(k,N)^CUE(b) <= H^k/k!.                            (4)

The upper bound follows by expanding (sum lambda_j)^k: each product with distinct indices occurs k! times, and all remaining terms are nonnegative. Equation (3) is the standard finite CUE/Toeplitz determinant identity; its elementary argument is given here, not a new priority claim.

## 3. Finite Bonferroni comparison

For h_1,...,h_N in [0,1], write e_k(h) for the elementary symmetric polynomials. For odd J,

    sum_(k=0)^J (-1)^k e_k(h)
       <= product_j(1-h_j)
       <= sum_(k=0)^(J-1) (-1)^k e_k(h).                     (5)

One proof is to realize h_j as the success probabilities of independent Bernoulli variables and apply the finite inclusion-exclusion inequalities. This auxiliary proof device changes no distribution in the BGS model. The statement is a pointwise polynomial inequality.

Apply (5), then average over alpha, to the deterministic spectrum and to CUE. Comparing their upper and lower bounds gives

    |Z_N^U(b)-Z_N^CUE(b)|
      <= sum_(k=2)^J |Delta_(k,N)(b)| + H^J/J!,               (6)

where Z_N^U(b)=integral product_j(1-b_N(theta_j-alpha)) d alpha.
The k=1 difference is exactly zero. This inequality needs no bounds on moments of the deterministic spectrum beyond the finitely many displayed Delta values. In particular, no uniform-in-N exponential count moment is assumed.

## 4. Compact local tests and positive Fourier smoothing

Fix f in C_c^infinity(R), f>=0, and put h=1-exp(-f). Then 0<=h<=c<1 and H=integral h.

Choose a nonzero eta in C_c^infinity(R), let a be its inverse Fourier transform, and normalize

    K(x)=|a(x)|^2/integral |a|^2,
    K_B(x)=B K(Bx),
    b_B=h*K_B.

Then b_B is nonnegative Schwartz, sup b_B<=c<1, b_B_hat is smooth and compactly supported, integral b_B=H, and ||b_B-h||_1 ->0 as B->infinity.

For large N both h_N and b_(B,N) lie in [0,1]. The elementary product Lipschitz inequality gives

    |Z_N^U(h)-Z_N^U(b_B)|
       <= sum_j integral_0^1
                    |h_N(theta_j-alpha)-b_(B,N)(theta_j-alpha)| d alpha
       <= ||h-b_B||_1.                                       (7)

The same bound holds after CUE averaging. If N exceeds the diameter of supp f, each eigenvalue label contributes at most one periodic point to that support, and

    Z_N^U(h) = E_alpha exp(-<f,Xi_(N,alpha)>).

Combining (6) and (7) proves the NONASYMPTOTIC certificate

    |L_N^U(f)-L_N^CUE(f)|
       <= 2||h-b_B||_1
          + sum_(k=2)^J |Delta_(k,N)(b_B)|
          + H^J/J!,                                         (8)

for every fixed B and odd J, once N is large enough for the displayed periodizations.

There is no exchange of the N limit with an infinite factorial expansion. For a prescribed accuracy, first choose B to control the smoothing error, then a finite odd J to control H^J/J!, and only then let N increase at these fixed choices. Numerical examples for H<=1,2,4 give tail below 10^-6 at odd J=11,15,21 respectively; these are reference-tail bounds, NOT bounds on the physical Delta terms.

## 5. Conditional local Sine2 theorem

**Theorem.** If (H_diag) holds, then Xi_(N,alpha) converges in distribution to the Sine2 point process. Every fixed local count moment is uniformly integrable and converges to the corresponding Sine2 moment. Every fixed compactly supported factorial correlation test also converges. An independent tightness or UI assumption is unnecessary under this all-order hypothesis.

**Proof of weak convergence.** At fixed B,J the middle term of (8) vanishes by (H_diag). Let B and J increase afterward. The finite CUE unfolded kernel, up to a gauge that cancels from determinants, is

    K_N(x,y)=sin(pi(x-y))/(N sin(pi(x-y)/N)).

Its correlation determinants converge locally to those of K(x,y)=sin(pi(x-y))/(pi(x-y)), and they are bounded by one (Gram/Hadamard). For compact h the coefficient convergence follows by dominated convergence; bound (4) and (5) give convergence of its Laplace functional by the same finite truncation. Hence L_N^CUE(f) tends to the Sine2 Laplace functional. Equation (8) gives the identical limit for U_N. Laplace functionals on nonnegative compact smooth tests determine the law of locally finite counting measures. Alternatively use (1) and Markov on increasing compact intervals for tightness, then the Laplace identity to identify every subsequential limit.

**Proof of local UI.** Fix a compact interval I. Choose a nonnegative bandlimited Schwartz b with sup b<1 and b>=c_I>0 on I. Such a b is obtained by squaring the inverse Fourier transform of a smooth bump, dilating its nonzero neighborhood to cover I, and multiplying by a sufficiently small positive constant. For N>diam I, different points in I have different original labels, even if some labels have coincident positions. Therefore pointwise

    (Xi_N(I))_r <= r! c_I^(-r) e_r(
                   b_N(theta_1-alpha),...,b_N(theta_N-alpha)).

Averaging and using (H_diag) and (4) shows that every fixed falling-factorial count moment is bounded uniformly in N. Stirling's identity expresses every ordinary count moment as a finite positive combination of these. In particular, the (r+1)st moment is uniformly bounded, proving UI of the rth power. Sine2 has no point at any deterministic endpoint, so count moments on intervals converge under weak convergence and UI. Compact continuous factorial tests are bounded by a fixed power of a local count, and converge in the same way. Extension to anchored Schwartz tests can also use the existing TRACE-004 occupation argument: Holder bounds masses in every translated unit cube by the same local moment, making Schwartz tails uniformly summable.

This is a sufficient theorem based on (H_diag), which is implied by the project's full H. It does not infer (H_diag) from a pair law, from one Fourier bandwidth, or from sublinear-time traces.

## 6. The first genuinely unproved physical coefficient

For real b, the pair coefficient is exactly

    Delta_(2,N)(b)
      = (1/N^2) sum_(m>=1) |b_hat(m/N)|^2
                     (|Tr U_N^m|^2-min(m,N)).                (9)

The m=0 term cancels. Positive and negative m have equal contributions, cancelling the 1/2! factor. This includes resonant and nonresonant times together. Equation (9) remains UNPROVED to tend to zero for the prescribed frozen U_N in this note. The stronger all-order coefficients are likewise unestimated. Equations (6)-(8) close the logical transition after these coefficients are controlled; they do not supply that control.

The earlier condition E_p(K)->0 for a separately selected resonance lattice is merely one sufficient deletion route, and is not inserted into this proof.

## 7. Assumption audit: what is, and is not, removed

- Fixed analytic symbol, odd N, and fixed tau=10^-80 define the studied family. They narrow generality; they are not unproved random-matrix hypotheses.
- Exact Weyl identities and correctly applied existing theorems are mathematical dependencies, not speculative assumptions.
- Prime-only results, shrinking kick amplitudes, logarithmic-time statements, and auxiliary random noise have their stated narrower domains. They cannot be substituted for the fixed all-odd-N problem.
- The main Heisenberg-scale signed coefficient estimates are substantive unresolved mathematical obligations. Calling them one hypothesis does not make them a small remaining step.
- Under full H, independent local UI, factorial-series tail, and local tightness premises can be removed as shown above. The existing TRACE-004 already addresses the every-bandwidth-to-Schwartz part; this note does not claim that extension as new.
- At weaker inputs (pair-only, bounded order, one fixed band, or weak convergence alone), UI cannot in general be dropped.
- No full audit of every p-adic or WKB source was performed in this continuation.

## 8. Sources, verification, and scope

Read project sources: mathematics/trace_to_local_statistics_bridge.md (exact Fourier/Mobius identity); mathematics/bandlimited_correlation_extension.md (occupation and Schwartz extension); record/claim_registry.md (separate conditional scopes); the supplied AUDIT_RU.md and CLAIM_LEDGER_RU.md (15 October 2026).

Primary background: A. Soshnikov, Determinantal random point fields, arXiv:math/0002099 (determinantal processes and convergence). The proof above spells out the finite CUE determinant and truncation operations needed here. Standard results are not attributed to this research as discoveries.

verify_closure.py checks exact rational Bonferroni brackets, a finite comparison bound, Stirling moment identities, the Fourier/Mobius coefficient identity, the finite CUE Toeplitz pair benchmark, and the integrated comparison bound on 15 spectra. Six tests use the exact BGS matrix formula at proxy tau=0.03, solely as finite algebra checks. These are NOT simulations of the frozen asymptotic coupling and NOT proofs of H. The verifier is run with python -O and uses explicit exceptions, not assert.

# Scale-normalized positive-quantization bound for the unchanged BGS kick

Date: 2026-10-15.

Status: a mathematical deduction from the quantified one-step Egorov and
finite-type estimates in BGS-FM-023, with a full derivation of the added
positive-quantization and scale-normalization steps. No external referee,
formal proof, priority claim, or BGS solution is asserted. Numerical controls
below do not prove the analytic bounds. No GitHub changes were made.

## 1. Model, notation, and statement

Keep the physical symbol and matrices exactly as in BGS-FM-001/FM-023:

    Q_N = exp(i tau N A_N),  U_N=Q_N M_N,  tau=10^-80,
    S = [[3,4],[2,3]],  N odd,
    T_N(a,b)psi(x)=e_N(b(x+inv2*a))psi(x+a).

The physical midpoint Weyl operator is W_N(a,b)=(-1)^(ab) T_N(a,b)
for integer labels, before reduction of labels. Symbols use
Op_N(exp(2pi i(kq+lp)))=W_N(l,k). These conventions are not interchanged.
Let rho_N(v) be the maximum norm of the centered integer representative
of v in (Z/NZ)^2. Thus rho_N(v)>=1 for v!=0. Put beta=1/65.

**Theorem A (all label scales, one kick).** There is C_tau, independent
of N,v,w, such that for all sufficiently large odd N and all v!=0,

    |(1/N) Tr[T_N(w)* Q_N T_N(v) Q_N*]|
                 <= min(1, C_tau rho_N(v)^(-1/65)).       (A)

The inverse-kick version has the same type of estimate. This controls an
individual matrix element of conjugation, not powers of this superoperator.
It is valid up to the largest centered labels O(N); it is not an Egorov
propagation theorem for time O(N).

**Theorem B (every prime cat orbit).** For p odd prime, set

    d_p=ord_p(S),
    F_p(v)=(1/p)Tr[Q_p* T_p(v) Q_p T_p(v)*].

For all sufficiently large primes,

    sup_(v!=0) (1/d_p) sum_(n=0)^(d_p-1) |F_p(S^n v)|
                 <= C_tau d_p^(-1/65)
                 <= C'_tau (log p)^(-1/65).              (B)

There is no split-prime condition and no premise d_p >> sqrt(p).
The same conclusion, with a changed constant, holds for the average over
0<=n<p. These are probes transported by the UNPERTURBED cat, acting on
one physical kick Q_p. They are not traces of powers of U_p.

## 2. Analytic inputs actually used

Let Psi=Phi_g^(-tau/(2pi)); replacing Psi with its inverse does not change
the argument. For a torus translation t_xi put

    H_xi = Psi t_xi Psi^(-1).

The four embedding coordinates are a_j=cos(2pi q), sin(2pi q),
cos(2pi p), sin(2pi p). Given input/output translations xi,eta, write

    u_j=a_j o H_xi, v_j=a_j o t_eta,
    B_j=u_j-v_j, B=(B_1,...,B_4), r=||xi||_(T,infinity)>0.

FM-023 supplies, using the actual primitive Fourier coefficients and the
explicit small-time remainder of this exact g:

(i) a fixed-step O(N^-2) Egorov estimate for smooth symbols, uniform in
all translation labels;
(ii) a finite-type estimate of order 16 for a selected component of
xi-eta+u(x+xi)-u(x), after division by r, where Psi(x)=x+u(x);
(iii) uniform smooth bounds on Psi and its inverse.

In particular, after the area-preserving change x -> Psi(x),

    area{|B|<=r epsilon} <= C_tau epsilon^(1/16),
                           0<epsilon<=1.                 (G1)

Embedding the torus in R^4 turns small chord distance into coordinate
distance modulo integers. Only a bounded number of integer lifts can occur.
The source's finite-type constants are proportional to |tau|r; |tau| is
fixed here and absorbed in C_tau. No uniformity as tau->0 is claimed.

Let K=Q_N T_N(v)Q_N*, L=T_N(w), D=K*L, and

    t_N(c)=(1/N)Tr[D Op_N(c)].

Exact translation Egorov and two uses of (i) give

    Op_N(u_j)D-D Op_N(v_j)=O_op(N^-2).                    (G2)

The product formula, cyclic trace and Fourier Cauchy-Schwarz yield

    |t_N(c B_j)| <= C_tau[N^-1 ||c||_(H3)
                              +N^-2 ||c||_(H2)].         (G3)

These are the quantified inputs reconstructed in FM-023 Sections 2--5.
The original source bounds t_N(chi) by a square root of bad-set area.
Sections 3--4 below replace that step with a positive quantizer, retaining
its error, and normalize the shrinking geometric scale BEFORE differentiating.

## 3. An explicit positive quantizer with a controlled Weyl error

This section does not assume positivity of Weyl quantization.
For odd N define the normalized, real, parity-even vector

    psi_N(x)=Z_N^(-1/2) exp(-pi center_N(x)^2/N),
    Z_N=sum_x exp(-2pi center_N(x)^2/N),
    R_N=|psi_N><psi_N|.

Gaussian sums/integrals give universal constants c,C>0 with

    c sqrt(N)<=Z_N<=C sqrt(N),
    sum_x center_N(x)^2 |psi_N(x)|^2 <= C N,
    ||(X-I)psi_N||^2 <= C/N.                             (P1)

For the last bound, on each non-wrapping edge use
|f(x+1)-f(x)|^2 <= integral_x^(x+1) |f'(s)|^2 ds for
f(s)=exp(-pi s^2/N); the wrapping edge connects equal endpoint values.
The integral over R of |f'|^2 is O(N^-1/2), and divide by Z_N.
The first two bounds follow by comparison with Gaussian integrals; a
fixed multiple of sqrt(N) interior lattice points gives the lower bound.

Let Z be coordinate modulation. The second bound in (P1) gives
||(Z^b-I)psi_N||<=C |b|/sqrt(N), while telescoping translations gives
||(X^a-I)psi_N||<=C |a|/sqrt(N). Since
W_N(a,b)=exp(pi i ab/N) Z^b X^a, this implies for |(a,b)|<=sqrt(N)

    ||(W_N(a,b)-I)psi_N||<=C |(a,b)|/sqrt(N).

Parity sends W_N(a,b) to W_N(-a,-b)=W_N(a,b)* and fixes psi_N.
Therefore t_(a,b)=<psi_N,W_N(a,b)*psi_N> is real and

    |1-t_(a,b)| = (1/2)||(W_N(a,b)-I)psi_N||^2
                   <= C |(a,b)|^2/N.                     (P2)

For larger integer labels, the same bound follows from |1-t|<=2.
All integer alias signs are retained; t_v W_N(v) is alias invariant.

For a scalar symbol a(q,p), define

    AW_N(a)=(1/N) sum_(q,p in Z/NZ)
       a(q/N,p/N) T_N(-q,p) R_N T_N(-q,p)*.               (P3)

It is positive for a>=0. Weyl orthogonality gives AW_N(1)=I and exactly

    (1/N)Tr AW_N(a)=(1/N^2)sum_(q,p) a(q/N,p/N),
    AW_N(e_(k,l))=t_(l,k) W_N(l,k).                       (P4)

Hence, using (P2),

    ||Op_N(a)-AW_N(a)||op
       <= (C/N) sum_n |n|^2 |a_hat(n)|
       <= (C/N)||a||_(H4).                               (P5)

In dimension two the Fourier Cauchy-Schwarz sum is finite at H4.
Also

    |(1/N^2)sum_grid a-integral a|
       <= C N^-4 ||a||_(H4),                             (P6)

by bounding the Fourier coefficients a_hat(Nk), k!=0.
For any unitary D and nonnegative smooth chi, positivity and (P5)--(P6)
therefore give the useful replacement for the square-root estimate:

    |(1/N)Tr[D Op_N(chi)]|
         <= integral chi + C N^-1 ||chi||_(H4).           (P7)

Indeed |Tr(D AW_N(chi))|<=Tr AW_N(chi). This is why the area, rather
than its square root, is legitimate here. The O(N^-1 H4) correction is
part of the proof and is NOT omitted.

## 4. Scale normalization and proof of Theorem A

Put s=dist_T,infinity(eta,xi). Smoothness and H_0=id imply

    ||a_j o H_xi-a_j o t_xi||_(C^k)<=C_(tau,k) r.         (G4)

This is the mean-value theorem in the translation parameter on compact
sets; higher x-derivatives obey the same bound. It is crucial to retain r.
Fix a sufficiently large constant A0 depending on the smooth map Psi.

### Near translations: s<=A0 r

The translation difference a_j o t_xi-a_j o t_eta is O_(C^k)(s).
Consequently beta_j=B_j/r has uniform C^k bounds for every required fixed k.
Equation (G1) states area{|beta|<=epsilon}<=C_tau epsilon^(1/16).
Choose a fixed smooth cutoff chi, equal to one below 1 and zero above 4,
and put

    chi_e=chi(|beta|^2/epsilon^2),
    c_(j,e)=(1-chi_e) beta_j/|beta|^2.

Then sum_j c_(j,e) beta_j=1-chi_e, and chain rule gives

    ||chi_e||_(H4)<=C_tau epsilon^-4,
    ||c_(j,e)||_(H3)<=C_tau epsilon^-4,
    ||c_(j,e)||_(H2)<=C_tau epsilon^-3.                   (G5)

Divide (G3) by r and use (G5). This yields

    |t_N(1-chi_e)|
       <= C_tau[(Nr)^-1 epsilon^-4
                      +(N^2 r)^-1 epsilon^-3].           (G6)

Equation (P7) handles the bad set:

    |t_N(chi_e)|
       <= C_tau[epsilon^(1/16)+N^-1 epsilon^-4].          (G7)

As r<=1 and epsilon<=1, all quantum errors are bounded by
C_tau (Nr)^-1 epsilon^-4. Since input v!=0 is a grid label, Nr>=1.
Set epsilon=(Nr)^(-16/65). The balanced terms equal (Nr)^(-1/65), so

    |t_N(1)|<=C_tau (Nr)^(-1/65).                         (G8)

For centered grid translations, Nr=rho_N(v).

### Separated translations: s>A0 r

For A0 sufficiently large, H_xi-t_xi has lift size at most (A0/2)r,
so H_xi and t_eta are separated on the torus by at least s/2.
Embedding coordinates give |B|>=c s. Also ||B||_(C^k)<=C_(tau,k) s.
Normalize beta=B/s and use c_j=beta_j/|beta|^2, whose needed derivatives
are uniformly bounded. Equation (G3), divided by s, gives

    |t_N(1)|<=C_tau/(Ns)<=C'_tau/(Nr)
                         <=C'_tau (Nr)^(-1/65).          (G9)

The modulus of t_N(1) is the modulus of the matrix element in (A).
Unitarity gives the additional bound one. This proves Theorem A, with
no restriction on the relative size of v and N beyond v!=0.

## 5. Consequence for divisor-grid operator compressions

For an odd D>=3 dividing N, let Pi be projection onto the D^2-1
nonzero Weyl labels (N/D)v. Each has centered size at least N/D.
The cat permutes this grid. Thus every matrix entry of Pi Ad(U_N) Pi
is bounded by C_tau(D/N)^(1/65). Retaining the full dimension loss,

    ||Pi Ad(U_N)Pi|| <= min(1,C_tau (D^131/N)^(1/65)).     (C)

For example D<=N^(1/262) gives O_tau(N^-1/130). This improves the
D^262/N power in FM-023 to D^131/N by the combined positive-quantization
and normalization argument. It is still a restricted compression, not
a gap for the full unitary superoperator Ad(U_N).

## 6. Elementary conic counting and proof of Theorem B

At odd primes, nonzero S-orbits have exactly d_p points. This follows
from its two distinct eigenvalues lambda,lambda^-1 of order d_p over
F_p or F_(p^2): S^j-I is invertible for 0<j<d_p.

S preserves Q(a,b)=a^2-2b^2. For any fixed centered first coordinate a,
the equation Q(a,b)=c modulo p has at most two b solutions. Therefore
for any nonzero orbit O and integer R>=1,

    #{v in O: rho_p(v)<=R} <= 2(2R+1) <= 6R.             (C1)

This includes c=0 on split primes; b^2=a^2/2 still has at most two
solutions for a fixed a. No nonsingular-shell assumption is needed.
If rho_1<=...<=rho_d are the sorted centered radii on O, (C1) implies
rho_j>=j/6. For 0<beta<1,

    (1/d)sum_(v in O) rho_p(v)^(-beta)
         <=6^beta/(1-beta) d^(-beta).                     (C2)

The diagonal matrix element in (A) is conjugate to F_p(v). Apply (A),
then (C2) with beta=1/65. This proves the first bound in (B).
Finally p^2 divides the nonzero integer det(S^d-I), while
|det(S^d-I)| < lambda^d, lambda=3+2sqrt(2). Hence
d>2 log p/log lambda, proving the logarithmic consequence.

Periodicity gives, for the p-step average, at most ceil(p/d) complete
cycles. Since d<=p+1, its ratio to the full-cycle average is at most
ceil(p/d)d/p <=1+d/p<=3. This proves the asserted O(p)-lag version.
The modulus is INSIDE the orbit sum throughout.

## 7. Where the attempt to close the spectral problem still stops

For a nonnegative bandlimited spectral test b, the outstanding pair defect
is still

    Delta_(2,N)(b)=N^-2 sum_(m>=1) |b_hat(m/N)|^2
                           (|Tr U_N^m|^2-min(m,N)).       (S)

Writing the kick superoperator in the Weyl basis as K and the cat
permutation as R, Ad(U_N)=K R and

    |Tr U_N^m|^2 = Tr_HS[(K R)^m].                        (S1)

Theorem A controls entries of K; Theorem B controls diagonal probes of
one kick along R-orbits. Neither theorem estimates the coherent closed
products of K R in (S1). No independence of successive factors is proved.
A normalized Fourier matrix has small entries and fourth power I, so an
entry estimate cannot silently become a power estimate. The present
physical assumptions do not identify our Q_N with that example; it only
invalidates that proposed inference.

Thus this continuation removes an actual arithmetic-size restriction and
extends a one-step matrix-element estimate to all input label scales. It
DOES NOT establish (S), Sine2, an all-order hierarchy, or deterministic
noise removal. The full signed sum (S), including all resonant and
nonresonant times together, remains the target.

## 8. Reproducibility and source boundaries

Run:

    OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 python -O verify_scaled_overlap.py --output verification.json

The program independently constructs the physical Jacobi generator and
metaplectic matrix. At proxy tau=0.03 and p=31,61,127,199,239,509 it checks
Weyl normalization, covariance, and the OTOC Fourier identity, evaluates
ALL nonzero S-orbits, and checks their exact integer small-box bounds.
It also checks (P3)--(P4), aliases, parity reality and finite multiplier
bounds at N=3,5,7,11,31. The global analytic constants in (P1)--(P7) are
proved above, NOT established by those finite runs.

The literal coupling 10^-80 is NOT simulated. Proxy diagnostics are not
proofs of applicability of the analytic finite-type estimate at tau=0.03,
nor tests of the literal asymptotic limit. No fitted decay exponent is used.

Read sources:
- ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/arithmetic_translation_exclusion.md,
  Sections 2--6; fetched source blob be8f73cc57b78088cd6e4c3f6162addaa75aedde.
- mathematics/phase_ward_identity.md (the different open spectral-overlap
  obligation; not supplied by the present Weyl-entry theorem).
- The supplied no-extra-UI THEOREM.md (the precise outstanding pair source).

Established background, not a claim of novelty: Rivas and Ozorio de Almeida,
The Weyl representation on the torus, arXiv:quant-ph/9904041; Saraceno and
Ozorio de Almeida, Translations and reflections on the torus,
arXiv:1808.00990. The positive-quantizer estimate required here is derived
explicitly instead of treating a general reference as proof of (A).

# Direct attack on the signed Heisenberg statistic

Date: 15 October 2026.

**Status: the fixed-coupling BGS conjecture is not proved here.** This packet contains a direct finite numerical probe of the required statistic, exact reference-ensemble algebra, and an explicit examination of the parameter-transfer step. It contains no assertion that numerical data, one-step dispersion, or a smoothed law establishes the deterministic microscopic limit. The repository was not changed.

## 1. Exact model versus numerical parameters

The mathematical target remains

\[
 U_N(\tau)=e^{i\tau N A_N}M_N,\qquad \tau=10^{-80},\quad N\ge3\text{ odd},
\]

with

\[
 r(q)=(1-\cos14\pi q)(1+\cos2\pi q),\qquad e^{i\alpha}=(-3+4i)/5,
\]
\[
 g(q,p)=\cos14\pi q+r(q)/10+r(q)\sin2\pi q/100-1/10
       +r(q+1/2)\cos(2\pi p-\alpha)/100.
\]

The code uses the exactly corresponding cyclic Jacobi matrix, with all inverse-of-two and inverse-of-eight operations taken modulo the odd dimension. In particular, its upper edge is

\[
 (A_N)_{x,x+1}=\frac{e^{-i\alpha}}{200}
 r\left(\frac{(x+2^{-1})\bmod N}{N}\right),
\]

and its diagonal is the coordinate part of g. The metaplectic factor is

\[
 (M_N)_{xy}=N^{-1/2}e_N[-8^{-1}(3x^2-2xy+3y^2)].
\]

The executed numerical parameters are **0.03 and 0.30, not 10^-80**. The matrix formula is unchanged but its coupling is changed for the diagnostics. The program does not claim the proxy classical maps satisfy the project's proven very-small-coupling Anosov hypotheses. No extrapolation to the literal fixed coupling is made.

## 2. The quantity tested

For a nonnegative smooth compactly supported weight w, the necessary pair comparison is

\[
 J_{N,w}(\tau)=N^{-2}\sum_{m\ge1}w(m/N)
 \left(|\operatorname{Tr}U_N(\tau)^m|^2-\min(m,N)\right).
\]

The four bands are (0.08,0.40), (0.75,1.25), (1.20,1.80), and (0.08,1.80). In a band (a,b) the weight is

\[
 w(t)=\begin{cases}
 \exp(1-1/(1-x^2)),&|x|<1,\\
 0,&|x|\ge1,
 \end{cases}\qquad x=(2t-a-b)/(b-a).
\]

The entire signed sum is evaluated. Resonance diagnostics are recorded separately but not deleted and not required to vanish. The last three bands test the reference plateau as well as the initial ramp.

Eigenphases are extracted by complex Schur decomposition, with residual and unit-circle checks. Traces of powers are independently checked against direct matrix powers at N=19,31,63. These floating-point residuals are numerical diagnostics, not interval-arithmetic enclosures of an asymptotic limit.

## 3. Exact dependence on the parameter and the attempted transfer

For fixed N, differentiation of the matrix exponential and trace cyclicity give

\[
 \partial_\tau\operatorname{Tr}U_N(\tau)^m
   =i mN\operatorname{Tr}(A_N U_N(\tau)^m).
\]

Consequently the full signed statistic has the exact derivative

\[
 \boxed{\partial_\tau J_{N,w}(\tau)=
 -\frac2N\sum_{m\ge1}m w(m/N)
 \operatorname{Im}\left[\operatorname{Tr}(A_NU_N(\tau)^m)
                  \overline{\operatorname{Tr}U_N(\tau)^m}\right].}
\]

All sums are finite. Integration over a real parameter interval is therefore legitimate at each fixed N. However, no estimate of this complete signed mixed trace sum, uniform enough for fixed parameter intervals, was proved.

The unconditional norm bound yields only

\[
 |J_{N,w}(\tau)-J_{N,w}(\sigma)|
 \le 2\|A_N\|_{\rm op}N |\tau-\sigma|
             \sum_m m|w(m/N)|
 =O_w(N^3|\tau-\sigma|).
\]

Here one may use the established model bound ||A_N||op <= 79/50. This gives a sufficient continuity scale o(N^-3). **It is not a necessity or optimality assertion**; a better model-specific estimate might exist. It does show that this argument does not transfer any numerical observation at a fixed proxy coupling to 10^-80. Qualitative algebraic nonvanishing does not bound the signed integral above either.

The derivative formula was checked against centered finite differences on the exact model at N=19,31,63. Such checks certify neither the missing uniform bound nor the N limit.

## 4. CUE calibration at every positive frequency

The exact mean is E_CUE |Tr V^m|^2=min(m,N). To avoid using the stable-range fourth moment outside its scope, the full covariance is used:

\[
 \boxed{\operatorname{Cov}(|p_m|^2,|p_n|^2)
 =\delta_{mn}\min(m,N)^2
 +2(N-\max(m,n))_+-(N-m-n)_+-(N-|m-n|)_+.}
\]

This is reference-ensemble algebra, not a distributional assertion about the deterministic U_N. In the stable range m+n<=N its last three terms sum to zero, consistently with the Diaconis--Evans moment identity.

For completeness it follows directly from the finite CUE determinant. Put F=sum t_j exp(i k_j theta), let P_N project onto Fourier labels 0,...,N-1, and expand

    log E_CUE exp(sum_j t_j p_(k_j))
      = Tr log(I+P_N(exp(F)-1)P_N).

For four labelled frequencies (m,-m,n,-n), the coefficient of t_1 t_2 t_3 t_4 is the sum over all 75 ordered set partitions. A partition into l blocks contributes (-1)^(l-1)/l times the trace of its product of Fourier shifts and projections. Since the total shift is zero, that trace equals

    (N - [maximum partial shift - minimum partial shift])_+.

Writing F_c=(N-c)_+ and taking m>=n, the four partition-length contributions are

| l | Contribution |
|---|---|
| 1 | F_0 |
| 2 | -F_0-2F_m-2F_n-F_(m+n)-F_(m-n) |
| 3 | 6F_m+2F_n+4F_(m+n) |
| 4 | -2F_m-4F_(m+n) |

Their sum is 2F_m-F_(m+n)-F_(m-n), the fourth joint cumulant. The additional Wick contraction is delta_(mn) min(m,N)^2, proving the covariance formula. Equal frequencies are handled by retaining all four labelled positions before combining equal arguments.

The reference standard deviation of J is computed from this full covariance. It is NOT a p-value, posterior probability, confidence interval for a deterministic theorem, or an assumption that U_N has a Haar law.

The separate exact-arithmetic checker verifies all 18,375 choices N=1,...,15 and m,n=1,...,35 by summing the 75 ordered partitions. This is an independent finite control on the displayed general derivation.

## 5. Outcome and unresolved step

The available one-step estimate bounds matrix elements of K=Ad(Q_N) in the Weyl basis. The true pair quantity instead involves

\[
 |\operatorname{Tr}U_N^m|^2
   =\operatorname{Tr}_{HS}[(K R)^m],
\]

where R=Ad(M_N) permutes the Weyl labels. Products contain correlated amplitudes along all closed paths. Pointwise decay of K, orbit averages of its diagonal probes, and an auxiliary heat law do not estimate those cyclic products.

The quantum-graph route was also checked against its averaging hypotheses. Its wave-number / independent bond-phase average is not supplied by the random choice of a single observation origin for this fixed spectrum. Applying such an ensemble theorem to the present U_N without a proved transfer would change the problem.

No bound proving J_N,w(10^-80)->0 was obtained in this pass. No higher signed factorial comparison is asserted. The full Heisenberg-scale dynamical input, rather than the deterministic Fourier bridge or the factorial truncation, remains the gap. Numerical agreement at proxy couplings is kept separate from this conclusion.

## 6. Reproduction and receipts

    OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python -O pair_probe.py
    python -O cue_covariance_check.py

The final probe is self-contained and has no imports from other research packets. Its default sizes are the completed sizes 127,251,509,777,1009. An additional N=2003 attempt exceeded its wall-time budget before producing a result; none is claimed. The 10 saved records through N=1009 were completed. A separate final self-contained smoke test ran N=19,31 successfully.

Source context, not a completed-proof claim:

- P. Diaconis and S. N. Evans, Linear Functionals of Eigenvalues of Random Matrices, Theorem 2.1, Berkeley report 577: https://statistics.berkeley.edu/sites/default/files/tech-reports/577.pdf . The stable moment theorem is used only within its stated range; the all-frequency covariance above is derived explicitly.
- Z. Pluhar and H. A. Weidenmuller, Quantum Graphs and Random-Matrix Theory, arXiv:1407.1148. Wave-number and bond-phase averaging are not identified with a single global origin average here.
- ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/heisenberg_pair_heat_formula.md, main e20825f15b62c403ddb52ef345aa48e339eda21d. Its exact smoothed formula does not estimate the unsmoothed statistic at the fixed initial matrix.
- Prior supplied scale-normalized-overlap THEOREM.md, 15 October 2026, explicitly distinguishes one-step Weyl dispersion from the open trace of powers.

# A quantitative route from parameter averages to the fixed-coupling BGS problem

Date: 15 October 2026.

**Status.** The finite parameter-bandwidth theorem, the de-averaging inequality, and the CUE reference moment bounds below are mathematical deductions. The required parameter-moment estimate for the prescribed BGS matrices is **NOT proved**. No BGS solution, external refereeing, formal verification, or priority claim is made. No repository files were modified.

## 1. Original operator and target are retained

Let A_N be Hermitian, M_N unitary, and assume a uniform bound

\[
\|A_N\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le C.
\]

Set

\[
U_N(t)=e^{itNA_N}M_N.
\]

For the physical BGS family N runs over odd integers, C may be taken as 79/50, the physical parameter is t_*=10^{-80}, and A_N,M_N are exactly the matrices used by the previous research packets. In particular, with

\[
r(q)=(1-\cos14\pi q)(1+\cos2\pi q),\quad e^{i\alpha}=(-3+4i)/5,
\]

A_N has diagonal

\[
\cos(14\pi x/N)+r(x/N)/10+r(x/N)\sin(2\pi x/N)/100-1/10
\]

and upper cyclic edge

\[
(A_N)_{x,x+1}=e^{-i\alpha}r(((x+2^{-1})\bmod N)/N)/200.
\]

The lower edge is its conjugate. Modular inverses are taken modulo odd N. Also

\[
(M_N)_{xy}=N^{-1/2}\exp\{-2\pi i8^{-1}(3x^2-2xy+3y^2)/N\}.
\]

The bound C follows from a diagonal absolute bound 1.54 and a total off-diagonal row bound 0.04. Small-size aliasing does not affect this bound.

Fix real w in C_c^infinity((0,B)), B finite. The signed pair target is

\[
J_{N,w}(t)=N^{-2}\sum_{m\ge1}w(m/N)
   (|\operatorname{Tr}U_N(t)^m|^2-\min(m,N)).                 \tag{1}
\]

All active times, resonant and nonresonant, are retained. No assertion that a selected sector separately vanishes is imposed.

## 2. Exact Fourier bandwidth in the coupling parameter

**Theorem 1.** As a function of real t, J_{N,w} is a finite exponential polynomial with angular Fourier frequencies contained in

\[
[-\Omega_N,\Omega_N],\qquad \Omega_N=1+2CBN^2.              \tag{2}
\]

Moreover

\[
\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}|J_{N,w}(t)|\le C_w N.                 \tag{3}
\]

No independence, spectral gap, Diophantine bound, or absence of eigenvalue collisions is used.

**Proof.** Write A_N=sum_j a_j P_j using its distinct spectral values; repeated eigenvalues are permitted. Then

\[
e^{itNA_N}=\sum_j e^{itNa_j}P_j.
\]

Every term in Tr U_N(t)^m has frequency N(a_{j_1}+...+a_{j_m}), whose absolute value is at most CmN. For real t the conjugate trace has the negative frequencies. Hence the squared trace has bandwidth at most 2CmN. The active m satisfy m<BN. The constant CUE subtraction only adds frequency zero. Finally |Tr U^m|<=N and there are O_w(N) terms in (1). This proves (2)-(3).

This is an exact bound on frequencies **in the scalar parameter t**. It is not an Egorov time window or a bound on frequencies of the classical symbol.

### All fixed factorial orders

For a nonnegative bandlimited Schwartz test b, with supp b_hat subset [-B,B], use the previous packet's scalar coefficient

\[
\Delta_{k,N}(t;b)=\frac1{k!N^k}
 \sum_{m_1+...+m_k=0}\prod_a\widehat b(m_a/N)
 \{D_{U_N(t)}(m_1,...,m_k)-\mathbb E_{\mathrm{CUE}(N)}D_V(m_1,...,m_k)\}.
                                                               \tag{4}
\]

The partition-Mobius formula expresses D as products of traces of positive and negative powers. For every partition, the sum of absolute block powers is at most sum_a |m_a|<=kBN. Expand inverse powers using U_N(t)^{-1}=M_N^{-1}e^{-itNA_N}. It follows that (4) is a finite exponential polynomial of bandwidth at most CkBN^2, and

\[
\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}|\Delta_{k,N}(t;b)|\le C_{k,b}N^{k-1}. \tag{5}
\]

The bound in (5) also follows directly from |D|<=N^k and the O_{k,b}(N^{k-1}) active lattice tuples. Thus the parameter bandwidth remains order N^2 at every **fixed** correlation order.

## 3. Local de-averaging theorem

Let F be a finite exponential polynomial with frequencies in [-Omega,Omega], Omega>=1, and sup_R |F|<=M. Let I be a fixed bounded interval and K a compact subset of its interior; write delta=dist(K,R\I)>0. Let q>1.

Choose chi in C_c^infinity(R), chi=1 on [-1,1], and let

\[
k(x)=\frac1{2\pi}\int e^{ix\xi}\chi(\xi)\,d\xi,\qquad
 k_\Omega(x)=\Omega k(\Omega x).
\]

Termwise integration of the finite exponential polynomial gives the exact identity F=k_Omega*F. Holder's inequality on I, and rapid decay of k on the complement, give for every A>0

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{t\in K}|F(t)|
\le \|k\|_{q'}\Omega^{1/q}
          \left(\int_I|F(s)|^qds\right)^{1/q}
   +C_A M(\Omega\delta)^{-A},\qquad q'=q/(q-1).
}                                                               \tag{6}
\]

The constants depend on the fixed kernel, q,A and the interval separation, not on the number or spacing of exponential frequencies. No lower bound on a difference of frequencies is required.

### Explicit reproducing kernel

The first kernel may alternatively be chosen with arbitrarily high finite regularity and an explicit formula. For an integer r>=1 set

\[
k_r(x)=\frac{\sin(2x)}{\pi x}
       \left(\frac{\sin(x/(2r))}{x/(2r)}\right)^{2r}.        \tag{7}
\]

Its Fourier transform is the convolution of 1_{[-2,2]} with the probability density of a sum of 2r uniform variables on [-1/(2r),1/(2r)]. Thus it is one on [-1,1] and zero outside [-3,3]. Consequently it reproduces the same bandwidth. Its explicit tail estimate is

\[
\int_{|x|\ge T}|k_r(x)|dx
 \le\frac{(2r)^{2r}}{\pi r}T^{-2r}.                         \tag{8}
\]

The inequality remains valid when the right-hand side is large. Replacing the last term of (6) by M(2r)^{2r}/(pi r)(Omega delta)^(-2r) gives a fully explicit finite-tail form. Increasing a fixed r controls any prescribed polynomial M_N.

### Application to the exact BGS family

Theorem 1 and (6) imply, for every fixed q>1 and every A>0,

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{t\in K}|J_{N,w}(t)|
\le C_{I,K,w,q}N^{2/q}
       \left(\int_I|J_{N,w}(t)|^qdt\right)^{1/q}
       +O_{I,K,w,A}(N^{1-2A}).
}                                                               \tag{9}
\]

Therefore

\[
\int_I|J_{N,w}(t)|^qdt=o(N^{-2})
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\sup_{t\in K}|J_{N,w}(t)|\longrightarrow0.                 \tag{10}
\]

In particular, a **physical parameter** sixth-moment bound

\[
\int_I|J_{N,w}(t)|^6dt=O_{I,w}(N^{-3})                     \tag{11}
\]

would give

\[
\sup_{t\in K}|J_{N,w}(t)|=O_{I,K,w}(N^{-1/6}).             \tag{12}
\]

More generally any O(N^{-2-epsilon}) qth-moment input gives O(N^{-epsilon/q}). Normalizing the integral by |I| only changes constants for a fixed interval.

For the literal parameter one may choose I=(t_*/2,3t_*/2), K=[3t_*/4,5t_*/4]. Both are fixed independently of N. The constants are allowed to depend on t_*, and the reconstruction kernel only becomes narrowly localized inside I after N^2 t_* grows large. No practical startup threshold or extrapolation from t=0.03 is asserted.

### All-order conditional closure

The same proof applied to (4)-(5) shows: if, for every fixed k and admissible b,

\[
\int_I|\Delta_{k,N}(t;b)|^6dt=O_{I,k,b}(N^{-3}),            \tag{13}
\]

then all these factorial coefficients converge uniformly on compact subsets of I to their CUE reference values. The previous packet's finite Bonferroni/Toeplitz comparison then gives the origin-averaged Sine2 process at each fixed t in int(I), including t_*. Its all-fixed-order moment control supplies local UI. No uniformity in growing k or growing Fourier support is required.

Equation (13) is a **sufficient, currently unproved physical input**, not an established assertion about U_N(t). This theorem does not turn an annealed mean, an almost-everywhere limit, or a CUE theorem into the desired input.

## 4. Why the rate matters: a sharp bandwidth obstruction

For any fixed t_* put

\[
F_N(t)=\left[\frac{\sin(N^2(t-t_*)/2)}{N^2(t-t_*)/2}\right]^2.
                                                               \tag{14}
\]

It is bounded by one, equals one at t_*, and has Fourier support in [-N^2,N^2]. For every fixed interval with t_* in its interior,

\[
\int_I |F_N(t)|^6dt\asymp N^{-2},\qquad F_N(t_*)=1.       \tag{15}
\]

In fact

\[
\int_{\mathbb R}\left(\frac{\sin x}{x}\right)^{12}dx
=\frac{655177}{1663200}\pi,
\quad
N^2\int_{\mathbb R}|F_N(t)|^6dt
=\frac{655177}{831600}\pi.
\]

The obstruction also occurs within the narrower class of **finite exponential polynomials**. With M=N^2, set

\[
G_N(t)=\left|\frac1M\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}e^{ij(t-t_*)}\right|^2.
\]

It is nonnegative, bounded by one, equals one at t_*, and has bandwidth M-1. For |t-t_*|<=pi it is bounded by min(1,pi^2/(M^2|t-t_*|^2)). For |t-t_*|<=1/M its value is at least cos(1/2)^2, by centering the phases in the geometric sum. Therefore the integral of G_N^6 on any fixed neighborhood of t_* of width less than one period is bounded above and below by positive constants times 1/M=N^{-2}. The verifier checks five such examples by exact integer coefficient convolution and independent alias-free Fourier quadrature.

Thus O(N^{-2}), without little-o improvement, does not force pointwise decay in the general bandwidth class. This is not claimed to be a BGS-matrix counterexample. It shows why an uncontrolled exceptional value cannot be dismissed solely from averaged convergence.

The natural CUE orders are second moment N^{-1}, fourth moment N^{-2}, sixth moment N^{-3}. The sixth is the first such even moment whose rate is strictly stronger than the N^{-2} bandwidth threshold. Nothing intrinsic about quantum dynamics singles out six; other quantitative qth-moment bounds can be used in (10).

## 5. CUE sixth-moment benchmark at arbitrary fixed bands

This section is ONLY a reference-ensemble result. It is not the physical assertion (11) or (13).

On the circle of length N, CUE is determinantal with projection kernel

\[
K_N(x,y)=\frac1N\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}e^{2\pi ij(x-y)/N}.
\]

For circular distance d_N, uniformly in N>=2,

\[
|K_N(x,y)|\le\min(1,(2d_N(x,y))^{-1}),\quad
\int_0^N|K_N(x,y)|^2dy=1,\quad
\int_0^N|K_N(x,y)|dy=O(\log(2N)).                           \tag{16}
\]

Consider an ordered fixed-k local U-statistic

\[
S_N=\sum_{j_1,...,j_k\text{ distinct}}
 H_N(x_{j_1},...,x_{j_k}),
\]

where H_N is the periodization of a translation-invariant Schwartz function of k-1 relative coordinates. For fixed k and fixed kernel,

\[
\operatorname{Var}(S_N)=O(N),\qquad
|\operatorname{cum}_r(S_N)|\le C_r N\log(2N)^{r-1},\quad r\ge2.
                                                               \tag{17}
\]

Here is the complete mechanism of the bounds. It is standard determinantal cumulant calculus, supplied to delimit the reference statement rather than claim novelty.

1. Expand a product of r copies of S_N by partitions of their kr point labels. Two labels in the same copy cannot be identified, since each copy is factorial. Each admissible global identification gives an integral against a determinantal correlation function in v<=kr distinct positions.
2. Expand each determinant into permutations. A diagram has (a) one fixed local hyperedge for each copy of H_N, (b) identifications from the label partition, and (c) directed K_N edges of the permutation. In the cumulant, the coefficient of such a diagram is the sum of partition-Mobius coefficients over partitions of the r copies coarser than its connectivity partition. This sum is zero unless the diagram connects all r copies. The determinant-permutation sign is unchanged by this cancellation.
3. Since the relative-coordinate kernel is Schwartz, for any sufficiently large fixed L it is bounded by a constant times a product of k-1 periodized envelopes h_N(x_i-x_k), with h(x)=(1+|x|)^(-L). These envelopes have uniformly bounded L1 and Linfinity norms. The hyperedge graph has c<=r connected components. Retain a spanning forest of its envelope edges and c-1 kernel edges connecting those components. Discard the remaining bounded factors. Integrating this spanning tree gives N times uniformly bounded L1 envelope factors and at most r-1 L1 norms of K_N. This proves the cumulant bound in (17). All sums of diagrams are finite for fixed k,r.
4. For r=2 the logarithm can be removed. If the two source copies share a point, their hyperedge graph is connected, and its integrable envelopes give O(N). If their positions are disjoint, a connected determinant permutation has at least two cross-edges between them. Integrate the relative anchor displacement s using
   \[
   \int_0^N |K_N(s+a)K_N(s+b)|ds\le1
   \]
   by Cauchy-Schwarz and (16). The internal relative coordinates are controlled by the L1 Schwartz envelopes. This also gives O(N), proving the variance assertion.

For the centered normalized real statistic Y_N=(S_N-E S_N)/N, (17) gives

\[
\kappa_2(Y_N)=O(N^{-1}),\qquad
\kappa_r(Y_N)=O(N^{1-r}\log(2N)^{r-1}).
\]

The exact sixth central-moment formula is

\[
E Y_N^6=\kappa_6+15\kappa_4\kappa_2+10\kappa_3^2+15\kappa_2^3.
\]

The first three terms are O(N^{-5}log^5 N), O(N^{-4}log^3 N), and O(N^{-4}log^4 N). The last is O(N^{-3}). Hence

\[
\boxed{E|Y_N|^6=O(N^{-3}).}                                 \tag{18}
\]

For (1), take the even extension W(x)=w(|x|), put phi=Fourier^{-1}(W), and periodize phi on the unfolded circle. The Fourier identity gives exactly

\[
J_{N,w}(V)=\frac1{2N}\left(
 \sum_{i\ne j}\phi_N(x_i-x_j)
 -E_{\mathrm{CUE}}\sum_{i\ne j}\phi_N(x_i-x_j)\right).
\]

Thus (18) proves the CUE bound E|J|^6=O_w(N^{-3}) for **every fixed compact band**, including bands above the plateau threshold m/N=1.

The same argument applies to each fixed scalar factorial coefficient M_{k,N}(b). Indeed its translation-invariant kernel is

\[
H_b(x_1,...,x_k)=\int_{\mathbb R}\prod_{a=1}^k b(x_a-t)dt,
\]

Schwartz in the relative coordinates, and M_{k,N} is its normalized ordered U-statistic with the factor 1/k!. Consequently the CUE sixth centered moment has the rate appearing in the unproved physical input (13).

### Independent stable-band calibration

When 6m_max<=N, the exact Diaconis--Evans trace moment theorem permits a shorter check. The moments through order six of J equal those of

\[
N^{-2}\sum_m m w(m/N)(E_m-1),
\]

where E_m are independent mean-one exponential variables. Its cumulants are

\[
\kappa_r=(r-1)!N^{-2r}\sum_m m^r w(m/N)^r.
\]

The same sixth-moment formula agrees with exact rational moment convolution. This shortcut is used by the verifier only in its proper stable range. The proof outside that range is the connected-determinant argument above, not an extension of Gaussian trace independence.

## 6. The physical parameter integral still needs a new estimate

The bandwidth reduction also gives an exact description of the missing moment. Write the actual real exponential polynomial as

\[
J_{N,w}(t)=\sum_\gamma a_\gamma e^{i\gamma t},\qquad
a_{-\gamma}=\overline{a_\gamma}.
\]

Collect the frequencies of its cube:

\[
J_{N,w}(t)^3=\sum_\lambda b_\lambda e^{i\lambda t}.
\]

For I=(c-L/2,c+L/2), exactly

\[
\boxed{
\int_I |J_{N,w}(t)|^6dt
=L\sum_{\lambda,\mu} b_\lambda\overline{b_\mu}
 e^{ic(\lambda-\mu)}
 \operatorname{sinc}\!\left(\frac{L(\lambda-\mu)}2\right),
}\tag{19}
\]

with sinc x=sin x/x. This is a positive Gram form in the complete complex coefficients b, not a sum of independent positive diagonal contributions.

In the A_N eigenbasis, coefficients of Tr U_N(t)^m are cyclic products of entries of the transformed M_N, grouped by occupation vectors. Formula (19) therefore retains the exact model and all signs. No approximation of e^{itN Op(g)} by Op(e^{itNg}) is used.

I checked the occupation expansion and (19) by independent finite matrix evaluations. I did **not** derive O(N^{-3}) or o(N^{-2}) for (19) in the physical family. Small frequency gaps can occur among the many occupation sums. Merely excluding exact algebraic relations does not bound the Gram form; replacing its off-diagonal terms by absolute values destroys the cancellations one is trying to establish.

The result is therefore a quantitative **conditional route** to the desired fixed parameter, not an actual deterministic universality theorem. It is also not a legitimate reason to import CUE's sixth moment into a one-parameter quantum family.

## 7. Relation to the attempted determinant route

The repository already contains a spectrum with exactly the CUE second covariance of its characteristic polynomial and a non-CUE local law. I therefore did not use a determinant-covariance criterion. Its determinant-ratio formula correctly preserves the missing information, but the required microscopic ratio estimate remains unproved.

The contribution of this packet is different: bounded generators give an exact N^2 parameter bandwidth, allowing a sufficiently rapid moment estimate on an interval to be turned into a fixed-parameter theorem without leaving an exceptional algebraic value unaddressed. This is elementary Fourier interpolation applied to the exact quantum family, not a newly established physical moment estimate.

## 8. Verification, sources, and limits

`verify_deaveraging.py` is self-contained and does not import previous research code. It checks:

- parameter-frequency expansions of the exact BGS matrix prescription at N=3,5,7, with 18 evaluations against direct matrix exponentials and powers;
- the sinc Gram formula for the sixth moment at N=3 against positive one-dimensional quadrature;
- nine Fourier reproduction tests for the explicit flat-top kernel, with a separate analytic tail bound;
- eighteen exact rational stable-CUE sixth-moment calculations by two independent formulas;
- nine exact rational connected-permutation/partition-Mobius determinant identities;
- the explicit sinc-spike integral and five exact finite Fejer-spike moment controls at the N^-2 scale.

All checks remain active under `python -O`. Floating-point controls are not interval-arithmetic certification, and none proves a limiting parameter moment for the physical coupling. N=7 is deliberately retained as an exceptional scalar-generator algebra check, not evidence for BGS.

Primary/background sources checked:

1. Diaconis--Evans, *Linear Functionals of Eigenvalues of Random Matrices*, Theorem 2.1, https://statistics.berkeley.edu/sites/default/files/tech-reports/577.pdf . Printed pages 3-4 were visually inspected. The theorem's weighted-degree condition is retained.
2. Soshnikov, *Determinantal random point fields*, https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0002099 . Determinantal and convergence background, not a theorem about the actual U_N.
3. Conrey--Farmer--Zirnbauer, *Howe pairs, supersymmetry, and ratios of random characteristic polynomials*, https://arxiv.org/abs/math-ph/0511024 . The result concerns Haar averages; none are imposed on the deterministic operator.
4. ChurilovMaksim/BGS, `supplement/fixed_map/weighted-secular/determinant_boundary.md` and `mathematics/heisenberg_pair_heat_formula.md`. These identify exact determinant reductions and explicitly unestimated physical terms.
5. Prior supplied `RESEARCH_STATUS.md` and `bgs_no_extra_ui_2026-10-15/THEOREM.md`. Their physical gap is retained; the latter supplies the already stated all-order Laplace closure.

**Final boundary:** equations (11), (13), and the corresponding o(N^-2) physical moment bounds remain unproved. The original all-odd-dimension Sine2 problem and general BGS are not closed by this packet.

# Exponentially large equal-edge contributions at Heisenberg times

Date: 15 October 2026.

**Status:** a self-contained exact counting formula and an asymptotic theorem
for dense, uniformly mixing unistochastic matrices. Application to the frozen
BGS family uses the explicitly stated project input FM-021 and its four-branch
parametrix. This is NOT a proof of the pair CUE limit, the signed all-order
hierarchy, or BGS. The repository has not been modified. No independent external
refereeing, formal verification, or world-priority claim is made.

The qualitative phenomenon of exponentially large periodic-orbit degeneracy
contributions and the need for compensating unitarity correlations was already
identified by Gregor Tanner (2000). The contribution of this note is the exact
rooted multiplicity normalization, a dense-mixing quantitative bound, and its
precise application boundary. The BEST theorem and directed matrix-tree theorem
are established mathematics, not discoveries of this project.

## 1. The complete, exactly counted family

Let U be an N by N unitary matrix and P_ab=|U_ab|^2. For a rooted closed word
x=(x_0,...,x_(m-1)), with x_m=x_0, set

    A(x)=product_(j=0)^(m-1) U_(x_j,x_(j+1)),
    nu_ab(x)=#{j: x_j=a, x_(j+1)=b}.

Then Tr U^m=sum_x A(x). Let c(nu) be the number of rooted words with edge
multiplicity matrix nu. Define

    B_(N,m)=sum_(x,y:nu(x)=nu(y)) A(x) conjugate(A(y)).

Identical edge multiplicities imply exactly identical complex amplitudes,
not just approximately equal actions. Therefore

    B_(N,m)=sum_nu c(nu)^2 product_ab P_ab^(nu_ab) >=0.       (1)

All rooted words, repetitions, loops, and nonprimitive cycles are included,
exactly once. This does not define the whole phase-balanced/vertex-occupation
sector; equal edges is a smaller, explicitly defined family.

## 2. Exact rooted BEST formula

An edge type of a nonempty closed word is a connected Eulerian directed
multigraph on its active vertices. Write

    r_a=sum_b nu_ab=sum_b nu_ba,  m=sum_a r_a.

Let t(nu) denote the common directed spanning-tree cofactor of
L=diag(r)-nu, restricted to the active vertices, with one row and column
deleted. The trees point toward the deleted root. Loops do not contribute to
L's diagonal. If there is only one active vertex, use the empty determinant 1.
Then

    c(nu)= m t(nu) product_(a:r_a>0)(r_a-1)!
                      / product_ab nu_ab!.                 (2)

For a disconnected support with edges in more than one component there is no
single closed word visiting all those edges, so c(nu)=0.

Proof of normalization: temporarily distinguish parallel directed edges.
With a specified initial vertex a and unrestricted initial edge, the last-exit
construction counts

    r_a t_a(nu) product_b(r_b-1)!

rooted labelled Euler tours. The final outgoing edges from all vertices other
than a form an arborescence toward a. Conversely, choose that arborescence and
orders of the other outgoing edges and follow unused edges in order. The last
exits prevent termination away from the root or stranding unused edges. In an
Eulerian graph all tree cofactors t_a agree (equivalently, the adjugate of the
balanced Laplacian has equal entries). Sum r_a over roots to obtain m.
Each rooted vertex word has exactly product nu_ab! assignments of parallel-edge
labels. Dividing by this number proves (2), including periodic rooted words.

Two elementary factorial inequalities give a useful bound:

    (r-1)! >= 2^((r-2)_+)  for r>=1,
    n! <= 2^(n(n-1)/2)     for n>=0.

Every graph realized by a closed word has t(nu)>=1. Put

    C_edge(x)=sum_ab binom(nu_ab(x),2),
    V_j(x)=#{a:r_a(x)=j}.

Since sum_a(r_a-2)_+=m-2N+2V_0+V_1, (2) yields

    c(nu(x)) >= m 2^(m-2N+2V_0(x)+V_1(x)-C_edge(x)).       (3)

## 3. An exact change of measure, not a new random quantum model

Let

    Z_(N,m)=Tr P^m=sum_x product_j P_(x_j,x_(j+1)).

When Z>0, define the probability of a closed word by

    Prob_loop(x)=product_j P_(x_j,x_(j+1))/Z.

This is only a convenient normalization of positive deterministic path weights.
It does not replace U with a random matrix. Equation (1) gives exactly

    B_(N,m)=Z E_loop[c(nu(X))].                             (4)

Jensen's inequality applied to (3) now proves the finite bound

    log(B_(N,m)/(m Z)) >= log(2) [m-2N
                      +2 sum_a Prob(R_a=0)
                      +  sum_a Prob(R_a=1)
                      -E C_edge],                         (5)

where R_a is the number of visits to a in the rooted m-word. Formula (5)
requires no mixing assumption.

## 4. Dense mixing forces an extensive multiplicity contribution

Assume, along N tending to infinity,

    max_ab P_ab <= C/N,
    ||P-J_N||_(2->2) <= rho <1,    (J_N)_ab=1/N,             (6)

with C and 0<rho<1 independent of N. P is doubly stochastic because U is unitary.
Let m=m_N satisfy m/N -> alpha in (0,infinity).

### 4.1. Normalization of the closed path law

Doubly stochasticity gives P^m-J_N=(P-J_N)^m. Hence

    |Z_(N,m)-1| <= N rho^m ->0.                             (7)

For every l>=1,

    (P^l)_ab <= C/N,                                       (8)

because multiplying a column bounded by C/N by a row-stochastic matrix
preserves that bound. Also |(P^l)_ab-1/N|<=rho^l.

### 4.2. Uniform Poisson limit for one vertex's visit count

For fixed k>=1 and fixed vertex a,

    E(R_a)_k = k!/Z sum_(0<=t_1<...<t_k<m)
                   product_(j=1)^k (P^(g_j))_aa,            (9)

where g_j=t_(j+1)-t_j, and the final cyclic gap is m-t_k+t_1.
For k=1 this means g_1=m.

Choose L_N=ceil(3 log N/|log rho|). The number of selected k-tuples having
any cyclic gap below L_N is O_k(L_N m^(k-1)). Their contribution, using (8),
is O_(k,C,alpha)(log N/N). On every remaining tuple,

    product_j(P^(g_j))_aa=N^(-k)(1+o(1)),

uniformly in a. Thus

    E(R_a)_k -> alpha^k uniformly in a.                     (10)

The same exact formula gives the bound

    E(R_a)_k <= (C m/N)^k/Z.

It allows dominated convergence in the falling-factorial expansion of
E z^(R_a), uniformly on compact subsets of the complex z-plane. Consequently

    Prob(R_a=0) -> exp(-alpha),
    Prob(R_a=1) -> alpha exp(-alpha),                       (11)

uniformly over a. These limits use ordinary dense Markov-loop mixing, not
spectral universality of U.

### 4.3. Directed edge repetitions have bounded mean

For two nonadjacent edge times separated by k, 2<=k<=m-2, their probability
of having identical ordered endpoints is

    (1/Z) sum_ab P_ab^2 (P^(k-1))_ba (P^(m-k-1))_ba
       <= C^3/(Z N^2).                                    (12)

Indeed sum_ab P_ab^2<=C. For adjacent times, equality of edges forces a
self-loop repeated twice; its probability is

    (1/Z) sum_a P_aa^2 (P^(m-2))_aa <= C^3/(Z N^2).

The same applies across the cyclic boundary. These formulas hold for m>=3.
Summing over pairs of times yields

    E C_edge <= C^3 m(m-1)/(2 Z N^2)=O(1).                  (13)

### 4.4. Exponential lower bound

Insert (7), (11), (13) into (5). Define

    h(alpha)=alpha-2+(alpha+2) exp(-alpha).

It is positive for every alpha>0: h(0)=0 and
h'(alpha)=1-(alpha+1)exp(-alpha)>0. We obtain

    liminf_(N->infinity) (1/N) log B_(N,m_N)
         >= h(alpha) log 2 >0.                             (14)

Equivalently, for every eta<h(alpha)log2, B_(N,m_N)>=exp(eta N)
eventually. The argument is uniform for m/N in a fixed compact subset of
(0,infinity), with the smallest h on that subset.

At alpha=1 the explicit lower exponential rate is

    (3/e-1) log 2 = 0.07183661174191525...

The use of 2^((r-2)_+) is not optimal. Keeping the full factorial in (2)
and using the uniformly bounded exponential moments of R_a improves the
rate to E[log((Poisson(alpha)-1)!)] with the value interpreted as zero at
0 and 1. No optimal exponential rate is asserted.

## 5. What unitarity already cancels, exactly

Let R_(N,m) be the sum over all remaining ordered path pairs, those with
unequal directed edge counts. Exactly,

    |Tr U^m|^2=B_(N,m)+R_(N,m).

Unitarity gives 0<=|Tr U^m|^2<=N^2, so

    -B_(N,m) <= R_(N,m) <= -B_(N,m)+N^2.                    (15)

Combining (14) and (15), R/B -> -1 exponentially fast in N (up to a
polynomial factor). This demonstrates a real collective cancellation at
Heisenberg times, but controls the final answer only to O(N^2), NOT to the
O(N) scale or the specific CUE reference needed by BGS.

There is an even more precise localization of this cancellation. Group by
vertex visits r=(r_1,...,r_N), and let

    H_r(U)=sum_(x:visits(x)=r) A(x).

If D=diag(exp(i theta_a)) with independent uniform phases, exact torus
orthogonality gives

    V_(N,m):=E_D |Tr(DU)^m|^2=sum_r |H_r(U)|^2 <= N^2.      (16)

The inequality is pointwise unitarity of DU before averaging. Thus the
cross-terms between different edge types WITHIN THE SAME vertex type already
satisfy

    V_(N,m)-B_(N,m)=-B_(N,m)+O(N^2).                        (17)

This identity is a diagnostic, not an assertion that phase averaging preserves
the original local law. Equal edges must not be confused with the entire
phase-balanced vertex sector. Independent phases on individual matrix entries
would instead produce B_(N,m), but that randomization generally destroys
unitarity and is not the diagonal-phase ensemble in (16).

All H_r have a finite formal generating function:

    H_r(U)=m [z^r] {-log det(I-D(z)U)},  |r|=m.              (18)

This follows from -log det(I-X)=sum_(m>=1)Tr(X^m)/m. Alternatively substitute
(2) and sum over edge counts with fixed row sums. Equation (18) resums the
multiplicities; it does not by itself bound the physical coefficients.

## 6. Explicit finite inequality for flat intensities

If |U_ab|^2=1/N exactly, then Z=1 and the m vertex labels are independent
uniform variables in the exact closed-word measure. For m>=3,

    Prob(R_a=0)=(1-1/N)^m,
    Prob(R_a=1)=(m/N)(1-1/N)^(m-1),
    E C_edge=m(m-1)/(2N^2).

Therefore, at every finite N and m>=3,

    B_(N,m) >= m * 2^F_(N,m),                              (19)

    F_(N,m)=m-2N+2N(1-1/N)^m
                     +m(1-1/N)^(m-1)-m(m-1)/(2N^2).

For N=m=1009 this lower bound exceeds 10^34, whereas unitarity bounds the
complete |Tr U^m|^2 by 1009^2. This is an exact-class illustration, not a
numerical threshold for the physical tau=10^-80 family.

## 7. Application to the exact frozen BGS matrices and its limits

Keep

    U_N=exp(i*10^-80*N*A_N) M_N

with the maintained full physical symbol, midpoint/raw dictionary, and all odd
dimensions. FM-021 in mathematics/position_chain_mixing.md gives the eventual
constant singular gap ||P_N-J_N||<=1/2, and the four-branch parametrix supplies
max|U_xy|^2<=C/N at the same fixed coupling. With these stated project inputs,
(14)-(17) apply to the actual family at every sequence m_N/N->alpha>0, including
composite dimensions. No artificial noise, weak-kick limit, parameter average,
prime-density condition or classical-to-quantum extension to time N is inserted
in the new theorem.

Those inherited analytic statements were read in this pass; they are not
re-certified by the finite checks. The core theorem (2), (5), (14) has a
self-contained proof from its displayed hypotheses. The source project
statements still have their original internal-review status, not independent
external certification.

The cyclic-rotation subfamily D_m studied in FM-021 has D_m~m. By contrast,
the COMPLETE equal-edge family B_(N,m) is exponentially larger and contains
non-cyclic reorderings. This is not a contradiction of D_m~m: D_m and B_(N,m)
are different subsets of the exact double sum. Fixed reconnection types or
marked endpoint cancellations cannot be extrapolated to the entire equal-edge
family without handling the multiplicities and compensating cross-types.

## 8. Remaining problem: the polynomial residual, not an exponential-sector estimate

The target remains, for each smooth compact w,

    N^-2 sum_(m>=1) w(m/N)
           [|Tr U_N^m|^2-min(m,N)] ->0,

and then the complete fixed-order factorial hierarchy. Neither (14) nor the
unitarity identity (15) estimates that residual at its required scale.

The quantitative lesson is not 'all different actions may be discarded':
doing so after retaining equal-edge degeneracies would leave an exponentially
incorrect answer. One must first keep/resum the phase relations imposed by
unitarity, including compensating terms inside each vertex-occupation class,
and then control the polynomial remainder using the physical phase structure.
The count formula (18) provides an exact, non-overcounting organization for
that attempt, but no signed CUE asymptotic is asserted for its coefficients.

An O(N) upper bound alone would still not determine the value min(m,N).
The current derivation establishes neither that bound for the specified
matrix nor the full required signed comparison. This is not closure of BGS.

## 9. Verification and sources

The autonomous Python verifier uses exact integer Bareiss determinants and
word enumeration to check (2), including loops and periodic words; it also
checks the exact multiplicity lower bound, complex trace regrouping, row-phase
Parseval via alias-free finite integration, and the explicit flat Jensen bound.
An independent rational Markov-loop calculation checks every factorial order
1 through 6 for N=3,m=6 and the repeated-edge probability bound.

Execution:

    OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python -O verify_edge_occupation.py

The completed run checked 16,284 words in 1,569 edge types, 18 exact rational
factorial-moment identities, and all displayed finite matrix controls.
Maximum complex trace-regrouping error: 3.31e-15.
Maximum row-phase Parseval error: 6.67e-15.
Proxy BGS matrices at tau=0.03 are used only in finite algebra checks; no
physical asymptotic result is inferred from them.

External primary sources consulted:

* G. Tanner, Spectral statistics for unitary transfer matrices of binary graphs,
  J. Phys. A 33 (2000), 3567-3585, DOI 10.1088/0305-4470/33/18/304;
  arXiv:nlin/0001025. Its qualitative exponentially large degeneracy contribution
  and unitarity-cancellation mechanism are explicitly acknowledged.
* M. Farrell and L. Levine, Multi-Eulerian tours of directed graphs,
  Electron. J. Combin. 23(2) (2016), P2.21, DOI 10.37236/5588;
  arXiv:1509.06237. Context for the classical BEST/last-exit counting mechanism.

Project inputs read:

* ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/position_chain_mixing.md, blob
  362ecbfe57040a99fb160bf757bc74259658dc63.
* ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/four_branch_parametrix.md, blob
  587cb2741921571bd327f93a443e3b2d2e92a690.

No whole-literature novelty audit or independent external referee acceptance
is claimed. These finite tests do not establish any N->infinity assertion;
the asymptotic proof is Sections 3-4 above.
