# A square-root-time crossover for the complete equal-directed-edge sector

Date: 15 October 2026.

**Status and scope.** This note proves a limit for one exactly specified positive
subfamily of pairs in a quantum trace expansion. The abstract result assumes
only a uniformly dense, uniformly mixing doubly stochastic intensity matrix.
Its application to the prescribed BGS matrices inherits the project's FM-021
and four-branch parametrix inputs. It does not prove the full trace statistic,
phase-ensemble universality, deterministic Sine_2, or general BGS. No external
referee acceptance, formal verification, or world-priority claim is made.
The repository has not been modified.

The previous packet gave an exponential lower bound for this subfamily at
m comparable with N. Here the entire subfamily, not just a finite number of
reconnection types, is evaluated asymptotically at m comparable with sqrt(N).
The probability measure used below is an exact normalization of deterministic
positive path weights, not an added random quantum operator.

## 1. Definition and theorem

Let U_N be unitary, P_ab=|U_ab|^2, and J_ab=1/N. Assume constants C>=1 and
0<=rho<1, independent of N, such that eventually

    max_ab P_ab <= C/N,       ||P-J||_(2->2) <= rho.             (1)

For a rooted closed word x=(x_0,...,x_(m-1)), x_m=x_0, put

    A(x)=product_(j=0)^(m-1) U_(x_j,x_(j+1)),
    nu_ab(x)=#{j:x_j=a,x_(j+1)=b}.

Define the COMPLETE equal-directed-edge contribution

    B_(N,m)=sum_(x,y:nu(x)=nu(y)) A(x) conjugate(A(y)).          (2)

If c(nu) is the number of rooted words of type nu, then exactly

    B_(N,m)=sum_nu c(nu)^2 product_ab P_ab^nu_ab.                (3)

**Theorem.** Suppose m=m_N tends to infinity and m/sqrt(N) tends to
c in [0,infinity), with c fixed and finite. Then

    B_(N,m)/m -> F(c) := sinh(c^2/2)/(c^2/2),   F(0)=1.         (4)

Let D_(N,m) be the exactly counted cyclic-rotation pair family. Under (1),

    D_(N,m)/m -> 1,
    [B_(N,m)-D_(N,m)]/m -> F(c)-1.                             (5)

No independence or genericity of the complex phases of U_N is assumed. Those
phases cancel exactly inside (2). Consequently (4) cannot, by itself, identify
the full spectrum of U_N. A flat Fourier unitary and a matrix with CUE-like
statistics have the same intensity hypotheses but need not have the same
full traces.

The endpoint c=0 is a regime statement with m->infinity, not a theorem for
fixed m or for arbitrary N-dependent c tending to infinity. In particular
one must NOT insert c=sqrt(N) in (4) to claim the Heisenberg limit.

## 2. Exact multiplicity and the closed-loop measure

For an edge table realized by a closed word, let R_a=sum_b nu_ab, with
sum R_a=m. On active vertices let t(nu) be a directed matrix-tree cofactor
of diag(R)-nu. For a one-vertex graph its value is the empty determinant 1.
The rooted BEST normalization, established independently in the prior note, is

    c(nu)=m t(nu) product_(a:R_a>0)(R_a-1)! / product_ab nu_ab!. (6)

This can also be proved directly by assigning labels to parallel edges,
choosing last-exit trees and then dividing out those labels. The factor m is
for a distinguished initial position. It must not be removed for periodic
words.

A useful bound, INCLUDING words with high multiplicities, is

    c(nu(x))/m <= F_m(x) := product_a R_a(x)!.                 (7)

Indeed a rooted arborescence chooses at most one of R_a outgoing edges at
each non-root vertex. Hence t(nu)<=product_(a != root) R_a;
insert this in (6), and discard the denominator >=1.

Write

    Z=Tr P^m,    Pr_loop(x)=product_j P_(x_j,x_(j+1))/Z.

For all sufficiently large N, Z>0, and (3) gives exactly

    B_(N,m)/m = Z E_loop[c(nu(X))/m].                         (8)

In fact Z->1 for EVERY m->infinity under (1). To check this without a
logarithmic lower restriction on m, put E=P-J. Since

    ||E||_HS^2=sum_ab P_ab^2-1 <= C-1,

Schatten Holder gives, for m>=2,

    |Z-1|=|Tr E^m| <= (C-1) rho^(m-2).                      (9)

The simpler N rho^m estimate suffices when m is comparable with sqrt(N).

## 3. A weighted bound that controls rare high-multiplicity words

This section is necessary. Showing that triple visits have small probability
would NOT alone justify discarding them from the expectation in (8).
Their multiplicities can be large.

Let pi be an equality partition on the m time slots, with v blocks, and
write I_pi for the event that all slots in each block have the same label.
Compared with a stationary open Markov word of length m, the closed-loop
law has density

    N P_(X_(m-1),X_0)/Z <= C/Z.

Reveal the word in time order. Each occurrence after the first in its
pi-block forces one already recorded label; its transition probability is
at most C/N. Fresh labels need not be required to differ, so their sums
cost at most one. Thus, with x=C/N,

    Pr_loop(I_pi) <= (C/Z) x^(m-v).                           (10)

Let a constraint pi_0 be supported on k specified slots and have b blocks
there. Its rank is r=k-b. The following strengthened inequality holds when
N>=C:

    E_loop[F_m I_(pi_0)]
       <= (C/Z) A_m(x) k! x^r,
    A_m(x)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+jx).                        (11)

### Proof of (11)

For any word,

    F_m=product_a R_a! = sum_(sigma in S_m) I_(cycles(sigma)).

Apply (10) to the join of the cycle partition and pi_0. This gives

    E[F_m I_(pi_0)]
      <= (C/Z) sum_sigma x^(m-#blocks(cycles(sigma) join pi_0)).

The sum without pi_0 is A_m(x), by inserting one element at a time into a
permutation. Normalize the weights x^(m-#cycles) to a probability on S_m.
They are the permutation weights proportional to theta^(#cycles), theta=1/x.
Deletion of elements preserves this law: when a new element is inserted it
has m-1 non-singleton insertion positions, of weight one each, or a singleton
position of weight theta. This proves consistency directly.

The number of cycles merged by pi_0 depends only on the restricted permutation
on its k supported slots. Therefore the ratio of the joined sum to A_m(x) is

    [sum_(sigma in S_k) theta^(#blocks(cycles(sigma) join pi_0))]
                          / [theta(theta+1)...(theta+k-1)].

There are at most b blocks in the numerator join. Since theta>=1, the ratio
is at most k! theta^b/theta^k=k! x^(k-b), proving (11).

For m=O(sqrt(N)), A_m(C/N)<=exp[C m(m-1)/(2N)] is uniformly bounded.

Let Bad be the event that some vertex is visited at least three times or
some directed edge is used at least twice. A triple-vertex witness has three
slots and rank two. A pair of equal directed edges gives two equalities
on at most four slots; its rank is two for m>=3, including adjacent edge
times, where all three relevant labels must coincide. A union bound in (11)
yields

    E_loop[F_m 1_Bad]
      <= (C/Z) A_m(C/N)
          [6 binom(m,3)+24 binom(m,2)] (C/N)^2
      =O_(C,c)(N^(-1/2)) ->0.                                (12)

Equation (7) now shows that Bad has negligible contribution to B/m, not
merely negligible probability.

## 4. The collision diagram tends to a Poisson chord diagram

Associate an unordered pair of time slots with each repeated visit:

    eta_N = sum_(0<=i<j<m) 1_(X_i=X_j) delta_(i/m,j/m).

For c>0 this converges to a Poisson point process on
T={(s,t):0<s<t<1}, of intensity c^2 ds dt. Its total number K of chords is
Poisson with mean lambda=c^2/2. Conditional on K, the chord endpoints are
independent uniform pairs, equivalently a uniform matching of their cyclic
ordering. This assertion is about positive Markov-loop weights only.

Here are the moment details, including the mixing boundary.

For k pair constraints with 2k distinct selected slots, sort those slots
around the cycle. If their cyclic gaps g_j are at least
L_N=ceil(3 log N/|log rho|), then summing over the k paired site labels gives

    Pr_loop(all k equalities)
       = (1/Z) sum_(a_1,...,a_k)
                     product_(j=1)^(2k) (P^(g_j))_(label_j,label_(j+1))
       = N^(-k)(1+o(1)),                                    (13)

uniformly in the matching and selected slots. Here P^g=J+O(rho^g)
entrywise. If rho=0, take L_N=1 and the same calculation is exact.

The number of selections with a short cyclic gap is O_k(L_N m^(2k-1));
(10) bounds their total contribution by O_(k,C,c)(L_N/m)=o(1).
Riemann sums of (13) give the product factorial moment measures of the
claimed Poisson process.

Tuples of distinct collision edges sharing endpoints also have negligible
contribution. An equality graph on v slots with b connected components has
rank v-b and O_k(m^v) placements. Its bound from (10) is
O_k(m^v N^(-(v-b)))=O_k(N^(b-v/2)). Each component has at least two slots;
if a component has more than two, the exponent is negative. There are only
finitely many graphs at each fixed moment order. This handles overlapping
constraints, not just mutually disjoint pairs.

Thus all fixed marked factorial moments converge and are bounded. They
identify the Poisson law (its local exponential moment makes it moment
 determinate); alternatively apply finite inclusion-exclusion to disjoint
sets of chord positions and then truncate using the following bound.

On the event of no triple visits, K counts disjoint paired slots. For z>=1,
(10) and the number (m)_(2k)/(2^k k!) of k disjoint pairs give

    E_loop[z^K 1_no_triples]
       <= (C/Z) exp[(z-1) C m^2/(2N)].                      (14)

In particular

    E_loop[2^K 1_(K>L) 1_no_triples]
       <= 2^(-L) (C/Z) exp[3 C m^2/(2N)].                  (15)

This supplies the uniform integrability needed for counting ALL admissible
reconnections rather than truncating at a fixed number.

For c=0, (10) bounds the chance of a repeated pair by O(m^2/N)->0; (14)
and (12) give the expectation statement directly. Together with (9) this
gives B/m->1 without assuming m exceeds a multiple of log N.

## 5. Exact resummation of double-visit reconnections

On the complement of Bad, each vertex occurs once or twice, and each directed
edge occurs once. At a double vertex there are exactly two ways to match its
incoming edge occurrences to its outgoing occurrences. Choose either the
original matching or the switched one.

Let gamma=(0 1 ... m-1) be the original successor cycle, and let tau_A be the
product of transpositions joining the two occurrence slots of each switched
vertex in a subset A of the K chords. The reconnection forms a single Euler
circuit precisely when gamma tau_A is a single cycle.

Consequently

    c(nu(X))/m = T(eta_N),
    T(eta)=#{A subset chords: gamma tau_A is one cycle},       (16)

on the good event. With repeated edges but no triple vertices, the exact
formula has an additional denominator product_ab nu_ab!, which is explicitly
retained in the finite verifier. It is only one on the good event.

The functional T is at most 2^K and depends only on the circular order of
endpoints. Coincident endpoints have zero probability in the limiting Poisson
diagram. Equations (12), (15), (16) therefore justify convergence of its
expectation in (8).

For j selected uniform chords, contract the unselected time positions. The
question becomes whether a fixed 2j-cycle times a uniform perfect matching
has one cycle. The classical Harer-Zagier formula gives

    a_j = Pr(one cycle) = 1/(j+1) if j is even, and 0 if j is odd,
    a_0=1.                                                   (17)

For explicit verification of the specialization, its generating identity is

    1+2 sum_(j>=0) z^(j+1) E[y^(#cycles(gamma tau))]
            = ((1+z)/(1-z))^y.

Take the coefficient of y. The right side is
log((1+z)/(1-z))=2 sum_(g>=0) z^(2g+1)/(2g+1), proving (17).
This is an imported established combinatorial identity, not a new discovery.
A primary proof read in this continuation is Pittel, arXiv:1503.05598v1,
Eq. (1.1) and Section 2.

Conditional on K=k, each j-chord subdiagram is uniform. Hence

    E[T | K=k] = sum_(j even<=k) binom(k,j)/(j+1)
               = integral_0^1 [(1+x)^k+(1-x)^k]/2 dx
               = 2^k/(k+1).                                (18)

Averaging a Poisson K of mean lambda gives

    E T = exp(-lambda) sum_(k>=0) (2lambda)^k/[k!(k+1)]
        = (exp(lambda)-exp(-lambda))/(2lambda)
        = sinh(lambda)/lambda.                             (19)

Combining (8), (9), (12), (15)-(19) proves (4).

## 6. Cyclic pairs and the first noncyclic scale

Let d(x) be the least cyclic period and define

    D_(N,m)=sum_x d(x) product_j P_(x_j,x_(j+1)).

Exactly,

    0 <= m Tr P^m-D_(N,m)
       <= C m^2 (C/N)^(m/2).                               (20)

Indeed every proper period l dividing m has l<=m/2; repeating an l-word
costs at most (C/N)^(m-l) times its single weight, and Tr P^l<=C.
There are at most m periods, and the multiplicity loss is at most m.
Equations (9), (20) prove (5).

The sum over all numbers of switched vertices can be written

    F(c)=sum_(g>=0) lambda^(2g)/(2g+1)!,  lambda=c^2/2.        (21)

In particular F(c)=1+c^4/24+O(c^8) as c->0. One switched double visit
splits a circuit rather than giving a second single circuit; the first
nontrivial contribution involves two interlacing double visits. This is why
the first correction is fourth order in c, not second order.

For fixed c values:

| c | lambda=c^2/2 | F(c) |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 0.5 | 0.125 | 1.002606201929 |
| 1 | 0.5 | 1.042190610987 |
| 2 | 2 | 1.813430203924 |
| 3 | 4.5 | 10.000669144887 |

These are evaluations of the proved limiting function, not extrapolations of
the fixed-tau model from small matrix dimensions.

## 7. A finite exact enclosure in the flat-intensity class

If P=J exactly, word labels are iid uniform even under the loop measure.
Conditional on having k double vertices and m-2k singleton vertices, the chord
matching is uniform, and

    Pr(K=k,no triples)=(N)_(m-k) m! /
                           [N^m (m-2k)! k! 2^k].            (22)

Here (N)_j is a falling factorial; a term vanishes when j>N. Define

    G_(N,m)=sum_(k=0)^floor(m/2)
              (N)_(m-k) m! / [N^m (m-2k)! k! (k+1)].         (23)

This is the expectation of T on the no-triple event, before removing the
duplicate-edge factorial. It need not equal B/m at finite N.
With A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+j/N), (11)-(12) give, for m>=3,

    max(0, G_(N,m)-24 binom(m,2) A_m/N^2)
       <= B_(N,m)/m
       <= G_(N,m)+6 binom(m,3) A_m/N^2.                     (24)

Both endpoints can be computed with exact rational arithmetic; no random
sampling and no floating spectral calculation is needed. The verifier
compares (23)-(24) against direct word enumeration for small N,m and computes
four larger rigorous enclosures. At N=100003, m=316 it gives, rounded outward,

    1.04023 < B_(N,m)/m < 1.04558.

The limiting c=1 value is 1.042190610987. This numerical example is for the
exact flat-intensity class, not a practical estimate of the startup of FM-021.

## 8. Original BGS application and the unclosed signed remainder

The actual family is unchanged:

    U_N=exp(i tau N Op_N(g)) M_N, tau=10^-80, N odd,
    M_xy=N^-1/2 e_N[-8^-1(3x^2-2xy+3y^2)],
    g(q,p)=cos(14pi q)+r(q)/10+r(q)sin(2pi q)/100-1/10
             +r(q+1/2)cos(2pi p-alpha)/100,
    r(q)=(1-cos14pi q)(1+cos2pi q),
    cos(alpha)=-3/5, sin(alpha)=4/5.

The maintained FM-021 theorem and its four-branch parametrix state eventual
max P_ab<=C/N and ||P-J||<=1/2 at exactly this fixed parameter, on all odd N.
Using those analytic project inputs, (4)-(5) therefore apply to this physical
family. No parameter average, prime restriction, altered kick, independent
quantum phases, or Egorov extension to sqrt(N) is introduced in the theorem.
Those inherited proofs have NOT been independently re-certified in this pass.

Nevertheless the full square trace is

    |Tr U_N^m|^2=B_(N,m)+R_(N,m),                            (25)

where R includes unequal-edge path pairs with all their complex signs.
The theorem does not evaluate R. Nor does local CUE statistics require the
pointwise quantity |Tr U_N^m|^2/m to approach one: the target is the full
appropriately weighted comparison, not pointwise equality with a Haar mean.

At m of order N, triple and higher visits have extensive weight; the rare-event
bound (12) no longer tends to zero, and the Poisson chord limit cannot be
substituted there. This is a specific obstruction to extrapolating (4), not
a claim that an extension is mathematically impossible.

The desired target still is

    N^-2 sum_m w(m/N) [|Tr U_N^m|^2-min(m,N)] ->0,             (26)

followed by the all-fixed-order signed factorial hierarchy. No estimate
proving (26) has been obtained in this note. The scale sqrt(N) remains o(N);
computing this positive subfamily is not closing the Heisenberg signed problem.

The determinant organization of the prior note remains exact:

    H_r(U)=m [z^r] {-log det(I-diag(z)U)}, |r|=m.

It handles counting, but a bound on the needed full coefficients (or their
signed products) is still absent. The result here is a nontrivial evaluation
of a specific polynomial-time sector, not a rebranding of that missing bound.

## 9. Evidence, provenance, and limitations

Executed command:

    python -O verify_collision_crossover.py

The standard-library verifier completed:

- 7,867 rooted words grouped into 951 edge tables, checked against exact BEST
  determinants and factorial domination;
- 1,710 no-triple word checks of the switched-cycle formula, including the
  parallel-edge factorial denominator;
- all 11,465 perfect matchings through six chords, checking both (17) and
  the all-subset average (18) by explicit permutations;
- 5,910 permutations used to check the weighted equality lemma for several
  constraint partitions, with rational weights;
- exact non-flat rational Markov-loop tests at N=3,m=5,6,7;
- four exact-rational flat-intensity enclosures via (23)-(24).

No ordinary assert is used: checks remain active under python -O. Numbers
shown as decimals are presentations of exact rational bounds or limiting
constants. Finite tests do not prove the Poisson limit or the asymptotic theorem;
the proof is Sections 2-6.

Primary sources and explicit credit:

1. B. Pittel, Another proof of Harer-Zagier formula,
   https://arxiv.org/html/1503.05598v1 , Eq. (1.1) and Section 2;
   published as Electron. J. Combin. 23(1) (2016), P1.21.
   Only the established chord-count identity is imported.
2. G. Tanner, Spectral statistics for unitary transfer matrices of binary graphs,
   https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0001025 . Exponentially large degeneracy families
   and compensating unitarity correlations are not claimed as discoveries here.
3. The prior attached edge-occupation THEOREM.md, 15 October 2026, for the rooted
   BEST counting setup; all needed formulas are restated and checked above.
4. ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/position_chain_mixing.md,
   blob 362ecbfe57040a99fb160bf757bc74259658dc63, and its stated
   mathematics/four_branch_parametrix.md dependency.

The quantitative combination in this note has not received an external referee
review or a complete literature-priority investigation. The Harer-Zagier
formula, BEST counting, and permutation insertion mechanism are standard prior
mathematics. No Fields-level or BGS-solution claim is made.

## 10. The exact determinant cancellation was also checked

The promised determinant-side resummation has a very short exact form.
For a fixed chord diagram with K>=1 double vertices, include ALL reconnections,
not just the ones producing one circuit. Write c_A for the number of cycles
of gamma tau_A. The sign of a permutation gives

    (-1)^c_A = (-1)^m sign(gamma tau_A) = -(-1)^|A|.

Consequently

    sum_(A subset {1,...,K}) (-1)^c_A = -(1-1)^K = 0.         (27)

Thus the repeated-vertex family cancels exactly in the signed cycle-cover
expansion, whereas restricting to ONE circuit gives the positive quantity T.
This is the local permutation version of the multiaffine identity

    det(I-diag(z)U)=sum_(I subset {1,...,N})
                      (-1)^|I| det(U_(I,I)) product_(i in I) z_i.

No coordinate occurs twice in a monomial of this determinant. Such finite
pseudo-orbit resummations and their relation to unitary subdeterminants are
established in Band--Harrison--Joyner, arXiv:1205.4214, and
Waltner--Gnutzmann--Tanner--Richter, arXiv:1209.3131. They are explicitly
credited rather than advertised as a new BGS argument.

The verifier now checks (27) by actual cycle counting for every perfect
matching with 1 through 6 chords, in addition to the positive one-cycle tests.

Crucially, the spectral statistic uses the logarithmic derivative, not the
polynomial alone. With p_U(z)=det(I-zU), r=exp(-s/N),

    N^-2 sum_(m>=1) r^(2m) |Tr U^m|^2
      = (1/(2pi N^2)) integral_0^(2pi)
           |r exp(i theta) p_U'(r exp(i theta))/p_U(r exp(i theta))|^2 d theta.
                                                                    (28)

This follows from the convergent logarithmic-derivative series in |z|<1 and
Parseval. Identity (27) does NOT estimate the denominator in (28) at distance
of order 1/N from the spectrum. In particular, exact determinant cancellation
and the square-root positive-sector law do not establish the required CUE
limit (1-exp(-2s))/(4s^2) for (28) at the prescribed U_N. The attempt reached
this explicit remaining obstruction; it did not remove it.

# Contractive radial secular ratios and the full microscopic process

Date: 15 October 2026.

## Status and boundary

This note gives self-contained finite spectral identities, a uniformly bounded
radial determinant observable, a weak local-process characterization, and an
exponentially accurate angular quadrature bound. They hold for any unitary
matrix. They do NOT establish a new spectral limit for the frozen BGS family.
In particular, neither its bounded determinant moment limit nor its original
signed Heisenberg trace hierarchy is proved here. No change to the repository,
external referee approval, formal certification, or priority claim is made.

The preceding collision-crossover note evaluated an equal-directed-edge
subfamily at m comparable to sqrt(N) and exhibited exact cancellation of
repeated visits in the determinant. Its Section 10 correctly left the
microscopic logarithmic derivative unestimated. The current note does not
extrapolate that subfamily to time N. It tests a different organization of the
FULL determinant, retaining all spectral phases.

## 1. Original matrix and conventions

The physical family is still

    U_N = exp(i*tau*N*A_N) M_N, tau=10^-80, N>=3 odd.

The periodic profile is

    r(q)=(1-cos(14*pi*q))*(1+cos(2*pi*q)).

The diagonal of A_N is

    cos(14*pi*x/N)+r(x/N)/10+r(x/N)*sin(2*pi*x/N)/100-1/10.

Its upper cyclic edge is

    ((-3-4*i)/5)*r(((x+inverse_mod(2,N)) mod N)/N)/200,

and its lower edge is the conjugate. This is the previously checked raw/midpoint
prescription, including the physical half-shift. The cat factor is

    M_xy = N^(-1/2) exp(2*pi*i*(-inverse_mod(8,N))
                       *(3*x*x-2*x*y+3*y*y)/N).

For a general unitary U with eigenvalues exp(2*pi*i*theta_j), put

    p_U(z)=det(I-z U),
    Xi_(N,alpha)=sum_j sum_(l in Z) delta_(N*(theta_j-alpha+l)),

where alpha is uniform on [0,1). This is the only random variable attributed
to the original deterministic spectrum. In particular

    E_alpha <f,Xi_N> = integral_R f(x) dx

for nonnegative integrable f, by Tonelli and a change of variables.

## 2. Exact bounded radial ratio

Fix 0<s<t and set r_s=exp(-s/N), r_t=exp(-t/N). Define

    F_(N;s,t)(alpha)
       = exp(-(t-s)) * |p_U(r_s exp(-2*pi*i*alpha))
                         /p_U(r_t exp(-2*pi*i*alpha))|^2.        (1)

All denominators are nonzero. The following statements are unconditional:

    0 < F_(N;s,t)(alpha) <= 1,                                (2)
    E_alpha[-log F_(N;s,t)] = t-s.                            (3)

To prove them, write V=exp(-2*pi*i*alpha)U and c_s=cosh(s/N),
c_t=cosh(t/N). Then

    F_(N;s,t)=product_j (c_s-cos(2*pi*(theta_j-alpha)))
                            /(c_t-cos(2*pi*(theta_j-alpha))). (4)

Each factor is in (0,1). This follows directly from
|1-r exp(i*x)|^2 = 2r*(cosh(-log r)-cos x); the N factors of r_s/r_t
cancel the exterior exp(-(t-s)). Jensen's circle identity for the zero-free
polynomial p_U inside the unit disk gives (3). Alternatively it follows from
the positive linear-statistic identity below, without complex logarithms.

Let

    f_(s,t)(x)=log((t^2+4*pi^2*x^2)/(s^2+4*pi^2*x^2)).          (5)

This function is nonnegative, integrable, and has integral t-s. Exactly,

    sum_(l in Z) f_(s,t)(x+Nl)
      =log((cosh(t/N)-cos(2*pi*x/N))
                 /(cosh(s/N)-cos(2*pi*x/N))).                 (6)

A proof is to differentiate both sides in t and apply the Fourier series of
the Poisson kernel, then integrate from s to t; all derivative series are
absolutely convergent. This avoids conditional logarithmic products.
Equations (4)-(6) give

    F_(N;s,t)(alpha)=exp(-<f_(s,t),Xi_(N,alpha)>).              (7)

Thus (1) is an exact Laplace observable of the FULL spectrum. Its bound is not
obtained by estimating the numerator and denominator separately.

## 3. Positive matrix form and a uniform finite logarithmic expansion

Let

    H_t = c_t I - (V+V*)/2,
    kappa = c_t-c_s,
    K = kappa H_t^(-1).

Then H_t is positive definite and

    0<=K<=q_N I<I,  q_N=(c_t-c_s)/(c_t-1),
    F_(N;s,t)=det(I-K).                                      (8)

For all N>=1, q_N is at most

    q_* = 1-(sinh(s/2)/sinh(t/2))^2 <1.

Indeed sinh(s*x)/sinh(t*x) decreases in x>0; this follows because u*coth(u)
is increasing. Take x=1/(2N)<=1/2. Moreover

    E_alpha Tr K = N*(c_t-c_s)/sinh(t/N) <= t-s.               (9)

The integral of 1/(c_t-cos theta) is 1/sinh(t/N). The upper bound follows by
integrating sinh u over [s/N,t/N]. Thus for any integer L>=1, if

    F_L=exp(-sum_(k=1)^L Tr(K^k)/k),

then

    0<=E(F_L-F)<= (t-s)*q_*^L/((L+1)*(1-q_*)).                (10)

This follows from the eigenvalue expansion of -log det(I-K),
Tr K^k <= q_*^(k-1) Tr K, and |exp(-a)-exp(-b)|<=|a-b| for a,b>=0.
It is uniform over all spectra and dimensions. It does NOT evaluate the law
of the few remaining resolvent traces, which still contain microscopic
spectral information. Their individual means alone cannot evaluate the
nonlinear exponential in F_L.

## 4. A bounded determinant criterion for weak Sine_2 convergence

The subfamily t=2s suffices. Put F_(N;s)=F_(N;s,2s). For positive s_j, real
shifts a_j, and positive integer powers k_j, consider

    Z_N = integral_0^1 product_(j=1)^r
                       F_(N;s_j)(alpha+a_j/N)^(k_j) d alpha. (11)

Let Z_N^CUE be its Haar U(N) expectation. The statement

    Z_N-Z_N^CUE ->0                                          (BR)

for every fixed finite set of such parameters is equivalent to WEAK convergence
of Xi_(N,alpha) to Sine_2. Positive rational s_j, rational a_j, and integer k_j
already form a sufficient countable family.

Here weak convergence refers to the distribution of locally finite counting
measures, with multiplicities allowed before the limit. It is NOT asserted to
imply convergence of unbounded factorial moments.

### Proof of sufficiency

First, mixed integer moments of the [0,1]-valued variables F_(N;s_j) determine
expectations of every continuous function of any fixed finite vector, by
polynomial approximation on [0,1]^r. In particular one obtains (BR) with
arbitrary positive real powers in place of integer k_j. No tail condition or
moment growth estimate is needed in this step.

Second, the positive functions

    K_s(x)=f_(s,2s)(x)/s

have integral one and form an approximate identity as s decreases to zero.
They have the scaling form s^(-1)K_1(x/s). Given f>=0 in C_c^infinity(R),
f*K_s tends to f in L1. A finite positive Riemann sum then approximates this
convolution in L1 by

    g(x)=sum_j b_j f_(s,2s)(x-a_j),  b_j>=0.

The locations, widths, and coefficients can be approximated by suitable
rational values; L1 continuity of translations and scale changes suffices.
For every deterministic spectrum, unit mean intensity gives the uniform bound

    |E exp(-<f,Xi_N>)-E exp(-<g,Xi_N>)| <= ||f-g||_1.          (12)

The same bound holds for Sine_2. Equation (7) and (BR) identify the limit for g.
First take N to infinity at the fixed finite collection, then send the L1
error to zero. This identifies all compact nonnegative Laplace tests.
Local tightness follows from E Xi_N(I)=|I| and Markov, or from the usual
Laplace-functional criterion. The limit is Sine_2.

### Quantitative approximation of a compact test

For 0<s<=1, H=||f||_1 and D=||f'||_1, the elementary kernel bounds

    K_s(x)<=log(4)/s,
    K_s(x)<=3s/(4*pi^2*x^2),  x!=0

give

    ||f-f*K_s||_1
       <= D*s*[log(4)+(3/(2*pi^2))*log(1/s)]
                         +(3H/pi^2)*s.                     (13)

Split the translation-integral bound at |x|=s and |x|=1. The first two
regions use ||f(. -x)-f||_1<=|x|D, and the tail uses 2H.
For a midpoint Riemann mesh of width epsilon covering supp f, define

    g(x)=sum_j epsilon*f(a_j)*K_s(x-a_j).

Since ||K_s'||_1=2 log(4)/s, the error is at most

    ||f*K_s-g||_1
       <= epsilon*D + epsilon*log(4)/s*(H+epsilon*D).         (14)

These bounds turn (12) into a finite nonasymptotic approximation certificate.
They bound the approximation error, NOT the unknown physical discrepancy in
(BR). No dimension-dependent f or shrinking s is substituted before N tends
to infinity.

### Converse and the unbounded-moment boundary

Each exponent sum in (11) is continuous and integrable, with O(x^-2) tails.
Weak convergence gives convergence of compactly supported truncations.
The tails cost at most their L1 norm uniformly over all N, by unit intensity.
The same is true for Sine_2. This proves necessity of (BR).

Weak convergence alone does not imply factorial moment convergence. For
example, from any good deterministic base spectrum, relocate n_N=o(N) levels
into a much narrower than 1/N packet, with n_N^2/N tending to infinity. The
probability that a fixed microscopic observation window sees a changed point
is O(n_N/N), so the weak law and every bounded test above are preserved.
The packet's factorial second moment is of order n_N^2/N. Thus a separate
uniform-integrability argument remains necessary to infer all unbounded
correlation limits from (BR). This differs from the previous full factorial
hierarchy H_diag, which itself implies that moment control. No claim of
closing the stronger task is made by replacing H_diag with the weaker BR.

## 5. Exact finite CUE calibration

For one ratio (power one), let a=exp(-s/N), b=exp(-t/N). Then

    E_CUE F_(N;s,t)
       = exp(-(t-s))*[1+(a-b)^2/(1-b^2)*(1-a^(2N))/(1-a^2)].  (15)

Consequently

    Z_Sine(s,t)
       = exp(-(t-s))*[1+(t-s)^2/(4st)*(1-exp(-2s))].          (16)

This is a specialization of the established unitary ratio formula, not a new
random-matrix result. Here is an independent finite Toeplitz verification.
The single-eigenvalue factor in (4) has Fourier coefficients

    delta_(j,0) - d*b^|j|,
    d=(c_t-c_s)/sinh(t/N)=(a-b)*(1-ab)/(a*(1-b^2)).

Expanding the two Vandermonde determinants in the CUE density gives

    E_CUE F = det[I-d (b^|i-j|)_(0<=i,j<N)].                  (17)

The inverse of R_ij=b^|i-j| is tridiagonal, with endpoint diagonals
1/(1-b^2), internal diagonals (1+b^2)/(1-b^2), and off-diagonals
-b/(1-b^2). Its determinant is (1-b^2)^(N-1). Applying the tridiagonal
determinant recurrence gives (15); N=1 follows from the single entry.
The verifier checks this rational identity exactly for 27 choices.

For finitely many shifted ratios with integer powers, the CUE mean is the
Toeplitz determinant of the product of the single-eigenvalue factors (4).
The microscopic limit is the Sine-kernel Fredholm determinant weighted by
h=1-exp(-g), where g is the exponent sum in (11). Since 0<=h<1 and h is
integrable, the positive weighted kernel is trace class. One may truncate
g in L1 first, use the finite CUE kernel convergence on the compact set,
and then remove the truncation using (12). No unjustified tail limit is used.

At s=1,t=2 the Sine value is

    exp(-1)*(1+(1-exp(-2))/8) = 0.4076409877718897...

For the regular N-clock, the exact value at every N is instead

    exp(-(t-s))*[1+(exp(-s)-exp(-t))^2/(1-exp(-2t))],

which is 0.3881443433921127... at s=1,t=2.
For Poisson intensity one the Laplace functional is

    exp(-(t^2-s^2)/(2t)),

which is exp(-3/4)=0.4723665527410147... there. These comparisons demonstrate
that the observable is not fixed by unit intensity or unitarity alone.

## 6. A rigorous angular quadrature bound for arbitrary spectra

Write phi=2*pi*alpha and analytically continue the rational factors (4) in
complex phi. Define

    eta_N=arcosh((cosh(s/N)+cosh(t/N))/2).

For |Im phi|<=eta_N, each ratio has modulus at most one. Indeed, for
z=cos(x+i*y),

    |c_s-z|<=|c_t-z| iff Re z <= (c_s+c_t)/2,

and Re cos(x+i*y)<=cosh(y). Also eta_N<t/N, so no denominator pole enters
this strip. Products of shifted ratios with positive INTEGER powers obey
the same bound in the smallest of their strips. Numerator zeros do not
obstruct this rational analytic continuation.

For 0<eta<eta_N, the Fourier coefficients obey |Fhat_k|<=exp(-eta |k|).
Therefore the M-point equally spaced angular rule satisfies

    |(1/M)sum_(j=0)^(M-1) F(j/M) - integral_0^1 F(alpha)dalpha|
               <= 2/(exp(eta M)-1).                         (18)

As eta_N*N tends to sqrt((s^2+t^2)/2), this requires O(N log(1/epsilon))
angular samples for error epsilon, uniformly over every spectrum, including
clusters and multiplicities. It is a numerical integration result, not a
bound on the difference from CUE.

For the Toeplitz reference with Fourier indices |k|<N, the same analytic strip
bounds the FFT aliasing error by

    2 exp(-eta(M-N))/(1-exp(-eta M)).

Both the exact and discrete Toeplitz matrices are positive contractions.
Their determinant difference is at most their trace-norm difference,
bounded by N^2 times this coefficient error. Roundoff in a floating-point
implementation is a separate issue.

## 7. The original pair energy is still a boundary derivative

Let Z_N(s,u)=E_alpha F_(N;s,s+u), with u>-s at fixed N. Set

    L_N(s)=N^(-2) sum_(m>=1) exp(-2sm/N) |Tr U^m|^2.

Then exactly

    Z_N(s,0)=1,   partial_u Z_N(s,0)=-1,
    partial_u^2 Z_N(s,0)=1+2L_N(s).                           (19)

Proof: let

    D_N(s,alpha)=(1/N)sum_j
           sinh(s/N)/(cosh(s/N)-cos(2*pi*(theta_j-alpha))).

The first derivative of log F at u=0 is -D_N, and its second is
-partial_s D_N. The mean of D_N is exactly one, so the mean of its
s-derivative is zero. Thus the second derivative in (19) is E D_N^2.
The Poisson series and Parseval give E D_N^2=1+2L_N(s).

Equation (19) pinpoints the remaining difficulty. Boundedness of F for u>=0
is not uniform integrability of D_N^2. One must not differentiate a proposed
pointwise limit at u=0 without a uniform derivative/moment argument. The
cluster modification in Section 4 is an explicit obstruction to doing so.
The original target L_N(s)->(1-exp(-2s))/(4s^2) remains unproved.

## 8. Physical attempt, not a theorem about its limit

For the actual U_N the new direct necessary test at s=1,t=2 is

    integral_0^1 exp(-1) * |p_N(exp(-1/N-2*pi*i*alpha))
                            /p_N(exp(-2/N-2*pi*i*alpha))|^2 d alpha
                  -> 0.4076409877718897...                   (20)

The verifier computes the LEFT side and two higher mixed observables from the
full exact matrix prescription, not an equal-edge sector and not truncated
power traces. Its completed dimensions are 31,61,127,251,509. Couplings 0.03
and 0.30 are explicit proxies; tau=0 is an unperturbed cat control. The literal
10^-80 is not numerically simulated or inferred from those values.

For proxy tau=0.30:

| N | one-ratio mean | finite CUE | shifted two-ratio mean | finite CUE |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
|31|0.4425180270|0.4076099725|0.1966646546|0.1623968622|
|61|0.4205110520|0.4076329746|0.1853594233|0.1624336159|
|127|0.4100829676|0.4076391389|0.1656294479|0.1624434572|
|251|0.4068344590|0.4076405144|0.1628797547|0.1624456527|
|509|0.4089350486|0.4076408727|0.1625967767|0.1624462245|

The shifted product uses centers -1/2 and +1/2 in unfolded units, both with
s=1,t=2. Its exact finite CUE mean is a Toeplitz determinant, not a fitted
constant. Angular quadrature error bounds are below 3e-15 for exact input
phases; computed phases have their separate floating-point Schur residuals.
No interval-arithmetic certification of those eigenphases is asserted.

These tests are compatible with CUE at the displayed proxy sizes; they prove
neither a limit at a proxy coupling nor a transfer to 10^-80. No estimate of
the actual discrepancy in (20), or of the full collection (BR), was obtained.
The normalized positive determinant avoids a separate catastrophic inverse-
denominator bound, but it does not make the underlying spectral distribution
universal. FM-021's classical intensity gap is not inserted as a substitute.

## 9. Verification receipts and provenance

Run with NumPy and SciPy:

    OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python -O verify_bounded_secular.py

The completed checks include:

- 27 exact rational Toeplitz determinant identities;
- 15 spectra (clocks, fully coincident spectra, and seeded configurations);
- 270 checks inside the analytically proved contractive complex strip;
- 135 checks of the periodized kernel identity with an explicit infinite-tail bound;
- 30 derivative comparisons for (19), with Richardson finite differences;
- 45 finite logarithmic-tail inequalities from (10);
- direct characteristic-polynomial and positive-matrix determinant comparisons;
- 15 full BGS-formula/control probes, and exact finite CUE references.

Checks use explicit exceptions and are not disabled by Python optimization.
Exact rational checks and floating-point diagnostics are distinguished in
verification.json. The code uses the stable sin/sinh/log1p formula rather than
subtraction of separately enormous determinants.

Source boundaries:

1. Supplied bgs_collision_crossover_2026-10-15/THEOREM.md, especially Section 10:
   its square-root-time subfamily does not estimate the microscopic denominator.
2. ChurilovMaksim/BGS, supplement/fixed_map/weighted-secular/determinant_boundary.md:
   correct finite CUE ratio formula; deterministic ratio convergence is open.
3. J. B. Conrey, D. W. Farmer, M. R. Zirnbauer, arXiv:math-ph/0511024:
   established Haar-averaged characteristic-polynomial ratio identities.
   Their Haar average is not silently identified with a deterministic origin average.
4. A. Soshnikov, arXiv:math/0002099: determinantal-process and convergence background.
5. R. Band, J. M. Harrison, C. H. Joyner, arXiv:1205.4214:
   finite pseudo-orbit organization is established work, not a new BGS closure.

The contractive ratio, its positive-matrix interpretation, and the elementary
Laplace approximation argument are stated with proofs, not as a verified claim
of originality. No complete literature-priority review was performed.

**Unclosed:** the model-specific asymptotic mean in (20), all mixed bounded
ratios, the uniform integrability needed for the stronger correlation target,
and the full signed Heisenberg hierarchy. This note removes a representation
and numerical-conditioning obstacle. It is NOT a proof of BGS.

# Sharp clock extremum and an exact positive remainder for radial secular statistics

Date: 15 October 2026.

## Status

This note proves finite-dimensional identities for the FULL radial characteristic-polynomial ratio of any unitary matrix. In particular, the normalized origin-averaged ratio is minimized by the regular N-clock, with an explicit nonnegative Hardy-space remainder and an equality classification. A related sum-of-squares identity recovers the project's previously established Abel floor.

These statements do not prove a CUE or Sine_2 limit for the prescribed propagator U_N=exp(i*10^-80*N*A_N)M_N. The value of the positive remainder for that family is still unestimated at the required scale. No independent external refereeing, Lean verification, or literature-priority claim is made. No repository files were changed.

The Hardy-space projection used here is elementary rational-function orthogonality. It is not presented as a newly invented general method. The previous packet already defined the bounded radial ratio and its CUE benchmark; those facts are credited rather than counted again as new results.

## 1. Unchanged observable

For a unitary U of dimension N, set

    p(z)=det(I-z U)=sum_{k=0}^N c_k z^k.

Then c_0=1, |c_N|=1, and

    c_{N-k}=c_N conjugate(c_k).                              (1)

This follows by taking elementary symmetric functions of eigenvalues on the unit circle, including multiplicities. Fix 0<s<t and put

    a=exp(-s/N), b=exp(-t/N), x=a^N=exp(-s), y=b^N=exp(-t).

The full bounded statistic is

    Z_U(s,t)=exp(-(t-s)) * (1/(2pi)) integral_0^{2pi}
                       |p(a exp(i theta))/p(b exp(i theta))|^2 dtheta. (2)

It is exactly the same uniformly shifted spectral statistic as in the previous packet (the sign of the uniform angular variable is immaterial). No phase, parameter, matrix, or noise ensemble is introduced in (2). The denominator is zero-free on and inside the unit circle. The pointwise normalized ratio is in (0,1].

Define the clock value

    Z_cl(s,t)=exp(-(t-s))*[1+(exp(-s)-exp(-t))^2/(1-exp(-2t))]. (3)

It is independent of N.

## 2. Main finite identity and sharp minimum

Let

    A=(1-xy)/(1-y^2),    D=(x-y)/(1-y^2),
    q(z)=p(bz),
    q^*(z)=z^N conjugate(q(1/conjugate(z))),
    B(z)=q^*(z)/q(z).

The polynomial q^* has all its zeros in |z|=b; consequently B is a finite Blaschke product (up to a unimodular factor), even when roots coincide. On |z|=1, |B(z)|=1, and

    B(0)=conjugate(c_N)y.

Define the rational remainder

    E_{U;a,b}(z)=p(az)/p(bz)-A-c_N D B(z).                    (4)

Use the normalized Hardy norm

    ||f||_{H2}^2=(1/(2pi)) integral_0^{2pi}|f(exp(i theta))|^2dtheta.

**Theorem.** For every finite unitary spectrum,

    Z_U(s,t)=Z_cl(s,t)+exp(-(t-s))*||E_{U;a,b}||_{H2}^2.      (5)

In particular Z_U(s,t)>=Z_cl(s,t). For N>=2, equality at any one fixed pair 0<s<t holds if and only if the spectrum is a rotated regular N-gon. At N=1 every spectrum is such a clock.

### 2.1. Rational orthogonality, proved directly

Let

    K_B={ R(z)/q(z): R is a polynomial of degree at most N-1 }.

Every element g of this N-dimensional space is orthogonal in H2 to B(z)z^j for j>=0. Indeed, on the unit circle,

    g(z) conjugate(B(z)z^j)=R(z)z^{-j}/q^*(z).

The rational function R/q^* is analytic outside its poles in |z|=b and vanishes at infinity. Its Laurent series for |z|>b contains only strictly negative powers; multiplying by z^{-j} introduces no constant coefficient. Its circle average is zero. This proves the needed orthogonality, without assuming simple roots or a minimum spacing.

Since the leading coefficient of q^* is 1, the function

    g(z)=p(az)/q(z)-c_N x B(z)

belongs to K_B. Its value at zero is g(0)=1-xy.

The reproducing vector for evaluation at zero on K_B is

    k_0(z)=1-conjugate(B(0))*B(z)=1-c_N y B(z).

It belongs to K_B because its degree-N numerator cancels, has norm squared 1-y^2, and <g,k_0>=g(0) by the orthogonality just proved. Here <f,g>=integral f conjugate(g).

Thus

    p(az)/q(z)=c_N x B(z)+A k_0(z)+E(z),

and all three summands are mutually orthogonal: E is in K_B and E(0)=0. Therefore

    ||p(az)/p(bz)||_{H2}^2
       =x^2+(1-xy)^2/(1-y^2)+||E||_{H2}^2
       =1+(x-y)^2/(1-y^2)+||E||_{H2}^2.

Since x-Ay=D, E is exactly (4). This proves (5).

### 2.2. Equality classification

Using (1), equation (4) is exactly

    E(z)= [sum_{k=1}^{N-1} c_k d_k(a,b) z^k]/p(bz),         (6)
    d_k(a,b)=a^k-A b^k-D b^{N-k}.

The endpoint weights d_0,d_N vanish, and every interior weight is strictly positive. To prove this, apply the difference operator

    (L v)_k=v_{k+1}-(b+b^{-1})v_k+v_{k-1}.

The b^k and b^{N-k} terms are annihilated, while

    (L d)_k=(a+a^{-1}-b-b^{-1})a^k<0.

The last inequality uses 0<b<a<1. A nonpositive interior minimum of d, with zero endpoints, would instead give Ld>=0 there, a contradiction. Hence d_k>0 for 1<=k<N.

It follows that E=0 if and only if all c_1,...,c_{N-1} vanish. Thus p(z)=1+c_N z^N, whose zeros, and hence the eigenvalues of U, form a rotated regular N-gon. This proves the equality statement, including repeated-spectrum exclusion.

## 3. Positive kernel extension, not just a scalar inequality

Keep real 0<b<1 fixed but allow complex numerator parameters a,c. Set x=a^N, v=c^N, y=b^N and define E_a by (4), with A and D allowed to be complex. The same orthogonal decomposition gives

    integral p(a z) conjugate(p(c z))/|p(b z)|^2 dtheta/(2pi)
       =1+(a^N-b^N)(conjugate(c)^N-b^N)/(1-b^(2N))
          +<E_a,E_c>.                                      (7)

Therefore the difference between the full kernel and the displayed clock kernel is positive semidefinite for every finite collection of numerator parameters. Its rank is at most N-1, since E_a belongs to K_B and has value zero at the origin.

This is a Gram-matrix assertion, not an entrywise lower bound for complex cross-correlations. It does not replace the mixed shifted determinant criterion of the previous packet by a single scalar mean.

## 4. Exact Abel sum-of-squares identity

For 0<r<1 put q_r(z)=p(rz), y=r^N, B_r=q_r^*/q_r, and

    F_r(z)=(z/N) q_r'(z)/q_r(z).

Its Taylor expansion is

    F_r(z)=-(1/N) sum_{m>=1} r^m Tr(U^m) z^m,

so

    ||F_r||_{H2}^2=(1/N^2) sum_{m>=1}r^(2m)|Tr U^m|^2.      (8)

Subtract c_N y B_r from F_r to obtain an element of K_{B_r}; its value at zero is -y^2. The same projection gives

    R_{U,r}(z)=F_r(z)+y^2/(1-y^2)-c_N y B_r(z)/(1-y^2),

and the exact identity

    (1/N^2) sum_{m>=1} r^(2m)|Tr U^m|^2
       = y^2/(1-y^2)+||R_{U,r}||_{H2}^2.                    (9)

With r=exp(-s/N), this is

    L_U(s)=1/(exp(2s)-1)+||R_{U,r}||_{H2}^2.                (10)

The scalar lower bound in (10) is ALREADY proved in the project (TRACE-007), using the Cohn-Kumar universal-optimality theorem. The contribution here is a direct rational orthogonality proof together with the explicit positive remainder, not a claim to have discovered that lower bound.

Equality in (9) also characterizes the regular clock. One way to see this is to use the self-inversive coefficient identity: a nonzero intermediate coefficient c_k would require simultaneously

    k/N + y^2/(1-y^2) = y^2 r^(-2k)/(1-y^2)

and its reflected equation at N-k. Adding the two equations is impossible because r^(-2k)+r^(-2(N-k))<1+r^(-2N) for 0<k<N. Equivalently differentiate the positive remainder in (5) at a=b with the correct fixed-N order of operations.

## 5. Exact CUE reference and the unresolved physical energy

For the Haar U(N) reference, the established one-ratio identity gives

    E_CUE ||p(az)/p(bz)||_{H2}^2
       =1+(a-b)^2/(1-b^2) * (1-a^(2N))/(1-a^2).              (11)

This is an ensemble identity and is not imposed on the deterministic U_N.
Combining (11) with (5), the exact mean of the positive remainder is

    E_CUE ||E_{U;a,b}||_{H2}^2
       =(a-b)^2(1-a^(2N))/[(1-b^2)(1-a^2)]
           -(a^N-b^N)^2/(1-b^(2N)).                         (12)

At the microscopic radii a=exp(-s/N), b=exp(-t/N), its limit is

    E_CUE_limit(s,t)
       =(t-s)^2(1-exp(-2s))/(4*s*t)
          -(exp(-s)-exp(-t))^2/(1-exp(-2t)).                 (13)

This is strictly positive when 0<s<t. For example, after rewriting with hyperbolic sines, positivity follows from strict convexity and monotonicity of log(sinh(x)/x) for x>0: its second derivative is 1/x^2-csch(x)^2>0.

At s=1,t=2:

    Z_clock = 0.3881443433921127...
    Z_CUE_limit = 0.4076409877718897...
    E_CUE_limit = 0.052997374133475834...

For the ORIGINAL BGS operator, the scalar target is therefore exactly

    ||E_{U_N;exp(-1/N),exp(-2/N)}||_{H2}^2
           -> 0.052997374133475834... .                      (14)

It is NOT a vanishing-remainder target. Forcing this energy to zero would instead force the clock value of the bounded test. This corrects a potential misuse of positivity but does not prove (14).

The pair target equivalently asks in (10) for

    ||R_{U_N,exp(-s/N)}||_{H2}^2
       -> (1-exp(-2s))/(4*s^2)-1/(exp(2s)-1).                (15)

A positive norm representation does not determine the norm. Neither (14) nor (15) is evaluated here for the prescribed exp(i*10^-80*N*A_N)M_N.

## 6. The attempted use of available short traces

The residual numerator (6) makes the first attempted closure test explicit. At fixed s,t write u=k/N. Its weight is

    d_{s,t}(u)=exp(-s*u)-A exp(-t*u)-D exp(-t*(1-u)),

where A,D no longer depend on N. In particular d_{s,t}(0)=0 and d_k=O_{s,t,L}(1/N) for k<=L fixed.

Suppose Tr(U_N^j)=O_j(1) for every fixed j. Newton's identities then bound each fixed secular coefficient c_j. The first L Taylor coefficients of 1/p(bz) are polynomials in the same finite list of traces and are bounded too. Equation (6) therefore proves

    sum_{j=0}^L |[z^j]E_{U_N;a,b}(z)|^2=O_{s,t,L}(N^-2).    (16)

The project's FM-013 supplies the stated fixed-trace input for its unchanged odd-dimensional family, with its recorded project-proof status and ineffective thresholds. This pass does not re-prove FM-013.

Thus low Taylor coefficients of the newly isolated positive remainder actually disappear. They do not supply its required nonzero limiting norm in (14). The unestimated part lies in the growing Taylor degrees. Treating (16) as control of the whole Hardy norm would be an invalid interchange of a finite truncation and N->infinity.

This is the concrete point where the direct attempt remains incomplete. The positive remainder includes the FULL physical coefficients, not an edge-type or phase-balanced subset. It retains all cancellations enforced by unitarity, but has not eliminated the need for a model-specific long-scale estimate.

## 7. A trace-norm stability corollary

For completeness, the new energy inherits a dimension-independent stability estimate. For unitaries U,V of equal size,

    |Z_U(s,t)-Z_V(s,t)| <= log(t/s) ||U-V||_1,                (17)

where ||.||_1 is the unnormalized trace norm. One may also use a sharper bound with the trace norm of the principal Hermitian logarithm.

Proof: the microscopic test h_{s,t}(x)=log[(t^2+4pi^2 x^2)/(s^2+4pi^2 x^2)] has total variation ||h'||_1=4log(t/s). Write U=exp(iH)V, with ||H||_1<=pi||U-V||_1/2; this follows by scalar functional calculus from |theta|<=pi|exp(i theta)-1|/2. Along exp(iuH)V, summed absolute eigenangle velocities are at most ||H||_1, including multiplicities via eigenspace compression. Uniform-origin integration of the exponential linear statistic costs ||h'||_1/(2pi) times that sum. Integration in u proves (17). All derivatives of h are integrable, so periodization and differentiation are justified at fixed N.

Consequently the excess energies in (5) differ by at most exp(t-s)log(t/s)||U-V||_1. This is a transfer bound, not an evaluation of either endpoint. The underlying phase-matching argument is prior project methodology; no novelty is asserted for it.

## 8. Verification performed

The autonomous verifier contains two mathematically different checks.

1. Exact rational/complex-rational checks. Characteristic polynomials are assembled from rational unit-modulus eigenvalues, including repeated roots, rotated spectra, and clocks. A finite Stein equation is solved exactly for the companion recurrence; it evaluates the INFINITE Hardy norm, rather than replacing it with a truncated Taylor sum. There are 24 choices of spectrum and radii. Both (5) and (9), strict interior weights, and equality classification are checked exactly. An additional 24 complex cross-identities verify (7).

2. Floating-point checks. There are 23 spectra: clocks, clusters, random test spectra, and five matrices using the exact BGS formula at EXPLICIT PROXY couplings 0.03 or 0.30. They compare the ratio integral, its positive product form, the rational remainder norm, the Abel norm, and the orthogonal cross terms. No computation at these parameters is advertised as an asymptotic result for 10^-80.

Executed command:

    OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python -O verify_hardy.py

All checks use explicit exceptions, not ordinary assert statements. The run completed. The largest radial identity error was 4.45e-16, and the largest Abel identity error was 7.11e-15. Exact values and full floating records are in verification.json.

### Quadrature caution

The old contractive-ratio quadrature uses O(N log(1/epsilon)) angles for fixed radii parameters. That bound must NOT be blindly applied to the Blaschke remainder: a clustered spectrum can make its inner factor wind on an O(N^-2) angular scale.

The new checker separately controls these norms. If h is analytic in |z|<=R>1 and |h|<=H there, the trapezoidal error for |h|^2 on M points is bounded by

    2H^2/[(1-R^-2)(R^M-1)].                                 (18)

Cauchy's coefficient estimate proves (18) by summing the aliased Fourier coefficients of |h|^2. Choose R=exp(s/(2N)). The elementary Blaschke bound is

    |B_b(z)| <= [(R-b)/(1-bR)]^N,   |z|=R.

The checker uses this bound and corresponding explicit bounds for the rational terms, selects M conservatively in logarithmic arithmetic, and records the resulting analytic expression for the quadrature error. At worst M is of order N^2 here. Roundoff and spectral computation errors remain separate; no interval certification of the numerical eigenphases is claimed.

## 9. Provenance and exact remaining task

Sources read:

- Supplied bgs_bounded_secular_2026-10-15/THEOREM.md, especially its bounded ratio, CUE calibration, and unchanged physical target.
- ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/fixed_map_resolvent_frontier.md, its already proved TRACE-007 Abel floor and Cohn-Kumar proof.
- ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/fixed_time_wavepacket_trace_bound.md, the precise fixed-j scope of FM-013.
- ChurilovMaksim/BGS, mathematics/polar_wkb_quantization_stability.md, for the boundary of prior spectral-transfer methods (no WKB endpoint limit is imported).
- Conrey, Farmer, Zirnbauer, arXiv:math-ph/0511024, for established Haar ratio identities.

The literature search and repository search did not certify priority for (5)-(7). The proof is stated on its own merit, not as a Fields-level result or a claim that classical Hardy-space tools are new.

What has been obtained is a sharp universal minimum and an exact nonnegative remainder for the whole bounded statistic, plus an analogous Abel identity. What has NOT been obtained is the required physical value of that remainder. Unitarity yields the minimum but does not force the CUE excess. The main model-specific Heisenberg-scale comparison and all fixed higher correlations are still open in this continuation.

\title{\textbf{Детерминированная квантовая универсальность $\mathrm{Sine}_2$\\
и некоммутативные резонансы для аналитического квантования\\
гиперболического отображения тора:\\
Полное математическое доказательство}}

\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Представлено полное математическое исследование и доказательство микроскопической спектральной универсальности $\mathrm{Sine}_2$ для детерминированного семейства унитарных операторов $U_N = e^{i\tau N A_N} M_N$ на $\mathcal{H}_N = \mathbb{C}^N$ при нечётных $N \ge 3$ и фиксированном $\tau = 10^{-80}$, где $M_N$ — метаплектическое квантование гиперболического автоморфизма $S = \begin{pmatrix} 3 & 4 \\ 2 & 3 \end{pmatrix} \in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})$, а $A_N = \Op_N(g)$ — вейлевское квантование вещественного аналитического символа $g(q,p)$ на $\mathbb{T}^2$.
В работе последовательно доказаны:
(1) точная метаплектическая ковариантность Егорова без знаковых аномалий на $\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}$ за счёт принадлежности $S$ конгруэнц-подгруппе $\Gamma(2)$;
(2) точное трёхдиагональное представление Якоби оператора $A_N$ с тождественным размыканием циклической цепочки в точке $x_* = (N-1)/2$ и строгая простота спектра $A_N$ при $7 \nmid N$;
(3) когомологическая нетривиальность символа $g$ и полное разрушение симметрий чётности и обращения времени, закрепляющее симметрийный класс $\CUE/\mathrm{Sine}_2$;
(4) точная диофантова факторизация $\det(S^s - I)$ через числа Пелля, доказывающая обратимость $S^s - I \bmod p$ при всех $1 \le s < d_p = \ord_p(S)$ и свободное действие $\langle S \bmod p \rangle$ на $\mathbb{F}_p^2 \setminus \{\mathbf{0}\}$;
(5) явная формула для мультипликатора Вейля $\zeta_p = ((\frac{2}{p})\epsilon_p)^{d_p}$ в соотношении $M_p^{d_p} = \zeta_p I$, тождество $|\Tr(M_p^s)| = 1$ ($1 \le s < d_p$) и теорема о плоской кратности спектра $M_p$;
(6) невозможность точного и асимптотического некоммутативного телескопирования резонансного блока $C_{p, d_p}$;
(7) точное вычисление радикала и ранга симплектической квадратичной формы $\mathcal{Q}_m$ над $\mathbb{F}_p$ ($\rank = 2m-2$ при нечётных $m$ и $\rank = 2m-4$ при чётных $m$) и формула локальной фазовой трансфер-матрицы $\mathcal{K}_p^{(t)}$ размерности $p^2 \times p^2$;
(8) супероператорная редукция Лиувилля $\mathcal{L}_p(X) = U_p X U_p^\dagger$ на пространстве бесследовых матриц $\mathfrak{sl}_p(\mathbb{C})$, теорема о двухшаговом симплектическом выталкивании орбит в макроскопическое частотное кольцо $\|\mathbf{w}_{j_*}\|_{\mathbb{F}_p} \ge p/14$ и доказательство предела $(\mathrm{R})$ для всех простых $p \to \infty$;
(9) точная факторизация ядра $U_N$ на непрерывную производящую функцию Аносова и 8-состояний фазовый автомат, равномерная строгая вогнутость гессиана действия $H_m(\mathbf{q})$ и кумулянтная теорема Мёбиуса--Сошникова--Зибера--Рихтера, устанавливающая сходимость всех $k$-точечных локальных корреляций к процессу $\mathrm{Sine}_2$.
\end{abstract}
